To be valid, a petition to reject an appointee must be signed by a number of eligible voters residing in the district equal to at least five percent of the total number of voters voting in the district at the most recent state general election, and must be filed within 30 days of the board's adoption of the resolution making the appointment. If a valid petition is filed according to the requirements of this paragraph, the appointment by the school board is ineffective and the board must name a new appointee as provided in paragraph (a).</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.09.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Meetings.</span></h2>
          <p>A majority of the voting members of the board shall constitute a quorum. No contract shall be made or authorized, except at a regular meeting of the board or at a special meeting at which all members are present or of which all members have had notice. Special meetings may be called by the chair or clerk or any three members upon notice mailed to each member at least three days prior thereto.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.09.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Policy making.</span></h2>
          <p>The board shall make, and when deemed advisable, change or repeal rules relating to the organization and management of the board and the duties of its officers.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.09.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Duties.</span></h2>
          <p>The board must superintend and manage the schools of the district; adopt rules for their organization, government, and instruction; keep registers; and prescribe textbooks and courses of study. The board may enter into an agreement with a postsecondary institution for secondary or postsecondary nonsectarian courses to be taught at a secondary school, nonsectarian postsecondary institution, or another location.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.09.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Removing board members.</span></h2>
          <p>The board may remove, for proper cause, any member or officer of the board and fill the vacancy; but such removal must be by a concurrent vote of at least four members, at a meeting of whose time, place, and object the charged member has been duly notified, with the reasons for such proposed removal and after an opportunity to be heard in defense against the removal.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.09.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">Publishing proceedings.</span></h2>
          <p>The board must cause its official proceedings to be published once in the official newspaper of the district. Such publication shall be made within 30 days of the meeting at which such proceedings occurred. If the board determines that publication of a summary of the proceedings would adequately inform the public of the substance of the proceedings, the board may direct that only a summary be published, conforming to the requirements of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/331A.01#stat.331A.01.10">331A.01, subdivision 10</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.09.11">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 11.<span class="headnote">Mailing summary of proceedings.</span></h2>
          <p>If a board of a district that has no newspaper with its known office of issue or a secondary office located within the boundaries of the district and no newspaper that is distributed to more than one-third of the residences in the district determines that mailing a summary of its proceedings would be more economical than publication of the proceedings and that it would adequately inform the public, it may mail a summary of its proceedings to each residence in the district that can be identified as a homestead from the property tax records and to each other residence in the district that the board can identify. The county must make the property tax records available to the board for this purpose. The board must keep a copy of the summary of the proceedings as part of its records. The decision of a board to mail summaries, rather than publish the proceedings under this subdivision shall be presumed valid, subject to challenge by a court action.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.09.12">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 12.<span class="headnote">Board to fix compensation.</span></h2>
          <p>The clerk, treasurer, and superintendent of any district shall receive such compensation as may be fixed by the board. Unless otherwise provided by law, the other members of the board shall also receive such compensation as may be fixed by the board. All members of the board may receive reimbursement for transportation at the rate provided for in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/471.665">471.665</a>. No board member or school district employee shall receive any compensation or benefits based on incentives or other money provided to the school district by or from a source of group insurance coverage referenced in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/471.6161#stat.471.6161.1">471.6161, subdivision 1</a>, except for a refund provided under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.75#stat.123B.75.10">123B.75, subdivision 10</a>, or a wellness plan that is mutually agreed upon by the district and the exclusive representatives of employees.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.123B.09.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1959/1/71/#laws.4.15.0">Ex1959 c 71 art 4 s 15</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1965/0/434/#laws.0.1.0">1965 c 434 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1967/0/176/#laws.0.2.0">1967 c 176 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1967/0/713/#laws.0.1.0">1967 c 713 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/263/#laws.0.1.0">1973 c 263 s 1</a>,2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/690/#laws.0.1.0">1973 c 690 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1974/0/82/#laws.0.1.0">1974 c 82 s 1</a>,2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/162/#laws.0.24.0">1975 c 162 s 24</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/314/#laws.7.16.0">1983 c 314 art 7 s 16</a>,17; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/543/#laws.0.6.0">1984 c 543 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/0/122/#laws.0.1.0">1985 c 122 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/42/#laws.0.1.0">1987 c 42 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/266/#laws.2.9.0">1987 c 266 art 2 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/329/#laws.9.7.0">1989 c 329 art 9 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/562/#laws.7.3.0">1990 c 562 art 7 s 3</a>; art 8 s 20; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/499/#laws.9.2.0">1992 c 499 art 9 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/224/#laws.7.6.0">1993 c 224 art 7 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/374/#laws.0.17.0">1993 c 374 s 17</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/646/#laws.0.2.0">1994 c 646 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/3/#laws.16.13.0">1Sp1995 c 3 art 16 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.6.45.0">1998 c 397 art 6 s 45</a>-53,124; art 11 s 3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/101/#laws.0.1.0">1999 c 101 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/467/#laws.0.4.0">2000 c 467 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/279/#laws.0.3.0">2014 c 279 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/0/70/#laws.1.1.0">2015 c 70 art 1 s 1</a>,2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/161/#laws.2.1.0">2016 c 161 art 2 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/0/92/#laws.1.8.0">2017 c 92 art 1 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/112/#laws.2.2.0">2024 c 112 art 2 s 2</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.123B.095"><b>123B.095</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/161/#laws.2.2.0">2016 c 161 art 2 s 2</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.123B.10">
        <h1 class="shn">123B.10 PUBLICATION OF FINANCIAL INFORMATION.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.10.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Budgets; form of notification.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) Every board must publish revenue and expenditure budgets for the current year and the actual revenues, expenditures, fund balances for the prior year and projected fund balances for the current year in a form prescribed by the commissioner within one week of the acceptance of the final audit by the board, or November 30, whichever is earlier. The forms prescribed must be designed so that year to year comparisons of revenue, expenditures and fund balances can be made.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) A school board annually must notify the public of its revenue, expenditures, fund balances, and other relevant budget information. The board must post the materials in a conspicuous place on the district's official website, including a link to the district's school report card on the Department of Education's website, and publish a summary of the information and the address of the district's official website where the information can be found in a qualified newspaper of general circulation in the district.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.10.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Debt summary.</span></h2>
          <p>The board must also publish at the same time a summary of bonds outstanding, paid, and sold; a summary of orders not paid for want of funds; certificates of indebtedness for the year ending June 30; the statutory operating debt of the district as defined and certified pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.81">123B.81</a>; and the balance amount of the reserved fund balance reserve account for purposes of statutory operating debt reduction established pursuant to sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/126C.40">126C.40</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/126C.45">126C.45</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/126C.48">126C.48</a>, and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.22">124D.22</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.10.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Budget inspection.</span></h2>
          <p>A statement must be included in the publication that the complete budget in detail may be inspected by any resident of the district upon request to the chief school administrator.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.10.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Cost per pupil.</span></h2>
          <p>The board must also publish at the same time the average cost per pupil in average daily membership educated in that district in the preceding year. This computation must be made exclusive of debt service or capital outlay costs.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.123B.10.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/1011/#laws.0.1.0">1969 c 1011 s 1</a>-3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1971/0/690/#laws.0.1.0">1971 c 690 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1977/0/447/#laws.7.17.0">1977 c 447 art 7 s 17</a>-19; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/543/#laws.0.7.0">1984 c 543 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/1/1/#laws.9.1.0">1Sp1986 c 1 art 9 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/398/#laws.7.42.0">1987 c 398 art 7 s 42</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/130/#laws.0.37.0">1991 c 130 s 37</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/499/#laws.12.29.0">1992 c 499 art 12 s 29</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/224/#laws.13.28.0">1993 c 224 art 13 s 28</a>; art 14 s 7; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/374/#laws.0.24.0">1993 c 374 s 24</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/212/#laws.4.64.0">1995 c 212 art 4 s 64</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.6.106.0">1998 c 397 art 6 s 106</a>,124; art 11 s 3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2006/0/263/#laws.4.1.0">2006 c 263 art 4 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/146/#laws.5.1.0">2007 c 146 art 5 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/88/#laws.3.1.0">2009 c 88 art 3 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/96/#laws.5.1.0">2009 c 96 art 5 s 1</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.123B.11">
        <h1 class="shn">123B.11 IMPREST CASH FUNDS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.11.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Imprest fund.</span></h2>
          <p>The board may establish one or more imprest funds for the payment in cash of any proper claim against the district which it is impractical to pay in any other manner, except that no claim for salary or personal expenses of a district officer or employee shall be paid from such funds. The board must appoint a custodian of each such fund who shall be responsible for its safekeeping and disbursement according to law. Money for the operation of such fund shall be secured by a transfer from the general fund. A claim itemizing all the various demands for which disbursements have been made from the fund must be presented to the board at the next board meeting after the disbursements have been made. The board must act upon it as in the case of other claims and an order shall be issued to the custodian for the amount allowed. The custodian shall use the proceeds of the order to replenish the fund. If the board fails to approve the claim in full for any sufficient reason, the custodian shall be personally responsible for the difference.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.11.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Advances.</span></h2>
          <p>The board may authorize an imprest fund for the purpose of advancing money to officers or employees to pay the actual and necessary expenses of such officer or employee in attending meetings outside of the district. The board must appoint a custodian of such fund who shall be responsible for its safekeeping and disbursement according to law. At the first regular meeting of the board after such meeting, the custodian shall submit an itemized claim for the actual and necessary expenses incurred and paid. The board must act upon it as in the case of other claims and an order shall be issued to the custodian for the amount allowed. The custodian shall use the proceeds of the order to repay the amount advanced from the fund and make final settlement with the officer or employee. As an alternative the board may authorize travel advances if control is maintained by use of a travel advance account, the balance of which is supported by names of employees to whom money has been advanced.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.123B.11.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/631/#laws.0.1.0">1969 c 631 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1977/0/447/#laws.7.16.0">1977 c 447 art 7 s 16</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.6.54.0">1998 c 397 art 6 s 54</a>,124</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.123B.12">
        <h1 class="shn">123B.12 INSUFFICIENT FUNDS TO PAY ORDERS.</h1>
        <p>(a) In the event that a district or a cooperative unit defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123A.24#stat.123A.24.2">123A.24, subdivision 2</a>, has insufficient funds to pay its usual lawful current obligations, subject to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/471.69">471.69</a>, the board may enter into agreements with banks or any person to take its orders. Any order drawn, after having been presented to the treasurer for payment and not paid for want of funds shall be endorsed by the treasurer by putting on the back thereof the words "not paid for want of funds," giving the date of endorsement and signed by the treasurer. A record of such presentment, nonpayment and endorsement shall be made by the treasurer. The treasurer shall serve a written notice upon the payee or the payee's assignee, personally, or by mail, when the treasurer is prepared to pay such orders. The notice may be directed to the payee or the payee's assignee at the address given in writing by such payee or assignee to such treasurer, at any time prior to the service of such notice. No order shall draw any interest if such address is not given when the same is unknown to the treasurer, and no order shall draw any interest after the service of such notice.</p>
        <p>(b) A district may enter, subject to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/471.69">471.69</a>, into a line of credit agreement with a financial institution. The amount of credit available must not exceed 95 percent of average expenditure per month of operating expenditures in the previous fiscal year. Any amount advanced must be repaid no later than 45 days after the day of advancement.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.123B.12.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1959/1/71/#laws.5.6.0">Ex1959 c 71 art 5 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1965/0/69/#laws.0.2.0">1965 c 69 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1967/0/761/#laws.0.1.0">1967 c 761 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/3/#laws.1.6.0">1Sp1995 c 3 art 1 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.6.114.0">1998 c 397 art 6 s 114</a>,124; art 11 s 3</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.123B.13">
        <h1 class="shn">123B.13 LAND IN SETTLEMENT OF CLAIM AGAINST SURETY.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.13.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Power of board to accept.</span></h2>
          <p>When any district now has or asserts any claim or judgment against any sureties on the bonds of any depository of its funds for the failure of any such depository to account for or pay over any such funds and the board or other governing body of the district determines that the claim or judgment, or some part thereof, is not collectible in cash, then any such board or governing body may by resolution determine to accept and receive, in complete or partial satisfaction or settlement of any such claim or judgment, lands or interest therein within this state and may acquire the same for and in the name of such district either by deed or deeds of conveyance from the owners, or as purchaser at execution sale or sales under any such judgment.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.13.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Title to be held by district.</span></h2>
          <p>The district must hold title to lands or interests so acquired. The district must sell each tract or portion as soon as there may be realized the fair value as determined by such board. Any such sale may be authorized by resolution of the board, and may be made for cash, or for part cash and the deferred balance secured by contract for deed or purchase money mortgage, on such terms as the board approves. Conveyances, contracts, or other instruments evidencing any sale shall be executed by the chair and the clerk of the board. Lands so acquired and held for resale shall be deemed public lands used for exclusively public purposes and as such shall be exempt from taxation.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.123B.13.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1959/1/71/#laws.5.7.0">Ex1959 c 71 art 5 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.6.115.0">1998 c 397 art 6 s 115</a>,124</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.123B.14">
        <h1 class="shn">123B.14 OFFICERS OF INDEPENDENT SCHOOL DISTRICTS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.14.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Officer selection.</span></h2>
          <p>On the first Monday of January of each year, or as soon thereafter as practicable, the board must meet and organize by selecting a chair, clerk, and a treasurer, who shall hold their offices for one year and until their successors are selected and qualify. The persons who perform the duties of the clerk and treasurer need not be members of the board and the board by resolution may combine the duties of the offices of clerk and treasurer in a single person in the Office of Business Affairs. They may appoint a superintendent who shall be ex officio a member of the board, but not entitled to vote therein.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.14.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Chair.</span></h2>
          <p>The chair when present shall preside at all meetings of the board, countersign all orders upon the treasurer for claims allowed by the board, represent the district in all actions and perform all the duties usually incumbent on such officer. In case of absence, inability, or refusal of the clerk to draw orders for the payment of money authorized by a vote of the majority of the board to be paid, the orders may be drawn by the chair, and paid by the treasurer. A statement of the orders drawn, with a copy of such orders, shall be delivered to the clerk by the treasurer, or the office of the clerk may be declared vacant by the chair and treasurer and filled by appointment.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.14.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Official depository.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The treasurer shall deposit the funds of the district in the official depository.</p>
          <p>(b) In addition to the authority for deposit of district money pursuant to paragraph (a) or other provisions of this chapter, the treasurer may deposit district money in the official depository in accordance with the following conditions:</p>
          <p>(1) The official depository is authorized by the treasurer to (i) arrange for the redeposit of the money into deposit accounts in one or more banks, savings and loan associations, or credit unions that are located in the United States, and (ii) serve as custodian for the district with respect to the money redeposited into such accounts.</p>
          <p>(2) The full amount of the redeposited district funds, plus accrued interest, if any, must be insured by the Federal Deposit Insurance Corporation or the National Credit Union Share Insurance Fund. Any entity serving as subcustodian for the official depository shall have had at least five years of general custodial experience.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.14.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Treasurer reports.</span></h2>
          <p>The treasurer shall make all reports which may be called for by the board and perform all duties usually incumbent on such officer.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.14.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Insufficient funds.</span></h2>
          <p>In the event that valid orders are presented to the treasurer for payment, and there are insufficient funds on hand to pay them, the treasurer shall receive, endorse and process them in accordance with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.12">123B.12</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.14.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Performance bond.</span></h2>
          <p>When the duty devolves upon any person employed by a board to receive money and pay it over to the treasurer of the district, the district must require a bond from such person and pay all premiums therefor. The amount of each bond shall be fixed by the board and the bond approved by it. The bond must be not less than $500 conditioned for the faithful performance of the duty and be filed with the clerk. In lieu of individual bonds, the district may prescribe and keep in effect a schedule or position insurance policy or blanket bond in such aggregate amount as the district determines, insuring the fidelity of such persons in the amount of not less than $500 for each such person.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.14.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Clerk records.</span></h2>
          <p>The clerk shall keep a record of all meetings of the district and the board in books provided by the district for that purpose. The clerk shall, within three days after an election, notify all persons elected of their election. By September 15 of each year the clerk shall file with the board a report of the revenues, expenditures and balances in each fund for the preceding fiscal year. The report together with vouchers and supporting documents shall subsequently be examined by a public accountant or the state auditor, either of whom shall be paid by the district, as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.77#stat.123B.77.3">123B.77, subdivision 3</a>. The board shall by resolution approve the report or require a further or amended report. By September 15 of each year, the clerk shall make and transmit to the commissioner certified reports, showing:</p>
          <p>(1) the revenues and expenditures in detail, and such other financial information required by law, rule, or as may be called for by the commissioner;</p>
          <p>(2) the length of school term and the enrollment and attendance by grades; and</p>
          <p>(3) such other items of information as may be called for by the commissioner.</p>
          <p>The clerk shall enter in the clerk's record book copies of all reports and of the teachers' term reports, as they appear in the registers, and of the proceedings of any meeting as furnished by the clerk pro tem, and keep an itemized account of all the expenses of the district. The clerk shall furnish to the auditor of the proper county, by September 30 of each year, an attested copy of the clerk's record, showing the amount of proposed property tax voted by the district or the board for school purposes; draw and sign all orders upon the treasurer for the payment of money for bills allowed by the board for salaries of officers and for teachers' wages and all claims, to be countersigned by the chair. Such orders must state the consideration, payee, and the fund and the clerk shall take a receipt therefor. Teachers' wages shall have preference in the order in which they become due, and no money applicable for teachers' wages shall be used for any other purpose, nor shall teachers' wages be paid from any fund except that raised or apportioned for that purpose.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.123B.14.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1959/1/71/#laws.4.16.0">Ex1959 c 71 art 4 s 16</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/9/#laws.0.27.0">1969 c 9 s 27</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1971/0/144/#laws.0.1.0">1971 c 144 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/492/#laws.0.7.0">1973 c 492 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1974/0/37/#laws.0.1.0">1974 c 37 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/162/#laws.0.25.0">1975 c 162 s 25</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/432/#laws.0.16.0">1975 c 432 s 16</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1978/0/706/#laws.0.13.0">1978 c 706 s 13</a>-15; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1978/0/764/#laws.0.31.0">1978 c 764 s 31</a>,32; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1979/0/334/#laws.6.8.0">1979 c 334 art 6 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/175/#laws.0.1.0">1981 c 175 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/314/#laws.7.18.0">1983 c 314 art 7 s 18</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/398/#laws.8.8.0">1987 c 398 art 8 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/562/#laws.8.21.0">1990 c 562 art 8 s 21</a>,22; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/265/#laws.9.34.0">1991 c 265 art 9 s 34</a>,35; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/224/#laws.9.22.0">1993 c 224 art 9 s 22</a>; art 12 s 15; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/3/#laws.9.19.0">1Sp1995 c 3 art 9 s 19</a>; art 16 s 13; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.6.55.0">1998 c 397 art 6 s 55</a>-61,124; art 11 s 3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/398/#laws.6.16.0">1998 c 398 art 6 s 16</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/254/#laws.0.9.0">2000 c 254 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/9/#laws.2.13.0">1Sp2003 c 9 art 2 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/96/#laws.5.2.0">2009 c 96 art 5 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/209/#laws.0.1.0">2012 c 209 s 1</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.123B.143">
        <h1 class="shn">123B.143 SUPERINTENDENT.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.143.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Contract; duties.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">All districts maintaining a classified secondary school must employ a superintendent who shall be an ex officio nonvoting member of the school board. The authority for selection and employment of a superintendent must be vested in the board in all cases. An individual employed by a board as a superintendent shall have an initial employment contract for a period of time no longer than three years from the date of employment. Any subsequent employment contract must not exceed a period of three years. A board, at its discretion, may or may not renew an employment contract. A board must not, by action or inaction, extend the duration of an existing employment contract. Beginning 365 days prior to the expiration date of an existing employment contract, a board may negotiate and enter into a subsequent employment contract to take effect upon the expiration of the existing contract. A subsequent contract must be contingent upon the employee completing the terms of an existing contract. If a contract between a board and a superintendent is terminated prior to the date specified in the contract, the board may not enter into another superintendent contract with that same individual that has a term that extends beyond the date specified in the terminated contract. A board may terminate a superintendent during the term of an employment contract for any of the grounds specified in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.40#stat.122A.40.9">122A.40, subdivision 9</a> or 13. A superintendent shall not rely upon an employment contract with a board to assert any other continuing contract rights in the position of superintendent under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.40">122A.40</a>. Notwithstanding the provisions of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.40#stat.122A.40.10">122A.40, subdivision 10</a> or 11, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123A.32">123A.32</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123A.75">123A.75</a>, or any other law to the contrary, no individual shall have a right to employment as a superintendent based on order of employment in any district. If two or more districts enter into an agreement for the purchase or sharing of the services of a superintendent, the contracting districts have the absolute right to select one of the individuals employed to serve as superintendent in one of the contracting districts and no individual has a right to employment as the superintendent to provide all or part of the services based on order of employment in a contracting district. The superintendent of a district shall perform the following:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) visit and supervise the schools in the district, report and make recommendations about their condition when advisable or on request by the board;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) recommend to the board employment and dismissal of teachers;</p>
          <p class="in">(3) annually evaluate each school principal assigned responsibility for supervising a school building within the district, consistent with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.147#stat.123B.147.3">123B.147, subdivision 3</a>, paragraph (b);</p>
          <p>(4) superintend school grading practices and examinations for promotions;</p>
          <p class="in">(5) make reports required by the commissioner; and</p>
          <p class="in">(6) perform other duties prescribed by the board.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.143.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Disclose past buyouts or contract is void.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) For the purposes of paragraph (b), a "buyout agreement" is any agreement under which a person employed as a superintendent left the position before the term of the contract was over and received a sum of money, something else of value, or the right to something of value for some purpose other than performing the services of a superintendent.</p>
          <p>(b) Before a person may enter into a superintendent's contract with a board, the candidate shall disclose in writing the existence and terms of any previous buyout agreement, including amounts and the purpose for the payments, relating to a superintendent's contract with another board. A disclosure made under this paragraph is public data.</p>
          <p>(c) The superintendent's contract of a person who fails to make a timely disclosure under paragraph (b) is void.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.123B.143.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1959/1/71/#laws.4.16.0">Ex1959 c 71 art 4 s 16</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/9/#laws.0.27.0">1969 c 9 s 27</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1971/0/144/#laws.0.1.0">1971 c 144 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/492/#laws.0.7.0">1973 c 492 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1974/0/37/#laws.0.1.0">1974 c 37 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/162/#laws.0.25.0">1975 c 162 s 25</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/432/#laws.0.16.0">1975 c 432 s 16</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1978/0/706/#laws.0.13.0">1978 c 706 s 13</a>-15; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/562/#laws.8.21.0">1990 c 562 art 8 s 21</a>,22; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/265/#laws.9.34.0">1991 c 265 art 9 s 34</a>,35; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/224/#laws.9.22.0">1993 c 224 art 9 s 22</a>; art 12 s 15; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/3/#laws.9.19.0">1Sp1995 c 3 art 9 s 19</a>; art 16 s 13; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.6.55.0">1998 c 397 art 6 s 55</a>-61,124; art 11 s 3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/398/#laws.6.16.0">1998 c 398 art 6 s 16</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/489/#laws.6.8.0">2000 c 489 art 6 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/6/#laws.1.5.0">1Sp2001 c 6 art 1 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/146/#laws.2.16.0">2007 c 146 art 2 s 16</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/96/#laws.2.34.0">2009 c 96 art 2 s 34</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/1/11/#laws.2.21.0">1Sp2011 c 11 art 2 s 21</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.123B.147">
        <h1 class="shn">123B.147 PRINCIPALS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.147.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Supervision of school building.</span></h2>
          <p>Each public school building, as defined by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/120A.05">120A.05, subdivisions 9, 11, and 13</a>, in an independent district may be under the supervision of a principal who is assigned to that responsibility by the board of education in that district upon the recommendation of the superintendent of schools of that district. If pupils in kindergarten through grade 12 attend school in one building, one principal may supervise the building.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.147.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Valid license required.</span></h2>
          <p>Each principal assigned the responsibility for the supervision of a school building shall hold a valid license in the assigned position of supervision and administration as established by the rules of the commissioner of education.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.147.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Duties; evaluation.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The principal shall provide administrative, supervisory, and instructional leadership services, under the supervision of the superintendent of schools of the district and according to the policies, rules, and regulations of the school board, for the planning, management, operation, and evaluation of the education program of the building or buildings to which the principal is assigned.</p>
          <p>(b) To enhance a principal's culturally responsive leadership skills and support and improve teaching practices, school performance, and student achievement for diverse student populations, including at-risk students, children with disabilities, English learners, and gifted students, among others, a district must develop and implement a performance-based system for annually evaluating school principals assigned to supervise a school building within the district. The evaluation must be designed to improve teaching and learning by supporting the principal in shaping the school's professional environment and developing teacher quality, performance, and effectiveness. The annual evaluation must:</p>
          <p>(1) support and improve a principal's instructional leadership, organizational management, and professional development, and strengthen the principal's capacity in the areas of instruction, supervision, evaluation, and teacher development;</p>
          <p>(2) support and improve a principal's culturally responsive leadership practices that create inclusive and respectful teaching and learning environments for all students, families, and employees;</p>
          <p>(3) include formative and summative evaluations based on multiple measures of student progress toward career and college readiness;</p>
          <p>(4) be consistent with a principal's job description, a district's long-term plans and goals, and the principal's own professional multiyear growth plans and goals, all of which must support the principal's leadership behaviors and practices, rigorous curriculum, school performance, and high-quality instruction;</p>
          <p>(5) include on-the-job observations and previous evaluations;</p>
          <p>(6) allow surveys to help identify a principal's effectiveness, leadership skills and processes, and strengths and weaknesses in exercising leadership in pursuit of school success;</p>
          <p>(7) use longitudinal data on student academic growth as 35 percent of the evaluation and incorporate district achievement goals and targets;</p>
          <p>(8) be linked to professional development that emphasizes improved teaching and learning, curriculum and instruction, student learning, culturally responsive leadership practices, and a collaborative professional culture; and</p>
          <p>(9) for principals not meeting standards of professional practice or other criteria under this subdivision, implement a plan to improve the principal's performance and specify the procedure and consequence if the principal's performance is not improved.</p>
          <p>The provisions of this paragraph are intended to provide districts with sufficient flexibility to accommodate district needs and goals related to developing, supporting, and evaluating principals.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.123B.147.history
			">

      <div class="section" id="stat.123B.19">
        <h1 class="shn">123B.19 FAILURE OF AUDITOR TO REPORT.</h1>
        <p>Any county auditor who shall fail to make to the commissioner any report of apportionment required by law shall forfeit, for the benefit of the school fund of the county, the sum of $50.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.123B.19.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1959/1/71/#laws.8.13.0">Ex1959 c 71 art 8 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.6.124.0">1998 c 397 art 6 s 124</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.123B.195">
        <h1 class="shn">123B.195 BOARD MEMBERS' RIGHT TO EMPLOYMENT.</h1>
        <p>Notwithstanding section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/471.88#stat.471.88.5">471.88, subdivision 5</a>, a school board member may be newly employed or may continue to be employed by a school district as an employee only if there is a reasonable expectation at the beginning of the fiscal year or at the time the contract is entered into or extended that the amount to be earned by that officer under that contract or employment relationship will not exceed $20,000 in that fiscal year. Notwithstanding section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.40">122A.40</a> or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.41">122A.41</a> or other law, if the officer does not receive majority approval to be initially employed or to continue in employment at a meeting at which all board members are present, that employment is immediately terminated and that officer has no further rights to employment while serving as a school board member in the district.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.123B.195.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1961/0/651/#laws.0.1.0">1961 c 651 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1965/0/806/#laws.0.1.0">1965 c 806 s 1</a>-4; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/26/#laws.0.1.0">1969 c 26 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/123/#laws.5.7.0">1973 c 123 art 5 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1977/0/55/#laws.0.1.0">1977 c 55 s 1</a>-3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1978/0/651/#laws.0.1.0">1978 c 651 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1979/0/20/#laws.0.1.0">1979 c 20 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/399/#laws.2.38.0">1986 c 399 art 2 s 38</a>-40; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/400/#laws.0.38.0">1986 c 400 s 38</a>-40; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/1/3/#laws.2.41.0">1Sp1986 c 3 art 2 s 41</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/65/#laws.0.1.0">1991 c 65 s 1</a>,2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/380/#laws.0.7.0">1992 c 380 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/522/#laws.0.42.0">1992 c 522 s 42</a>,43; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/224/#laws.9.43.0">1993 c 224 art 9 s 43</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/471/#laws.7.18.0">1996 c 471 art 7 s 18</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/269/#laws.0.1.0">1998 c 269 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.11.3.0">1998 c 397 art 11 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/241/#laws.6.3.0">1999 c 241 art 6 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/294/#laws.2.12.0">2004 c 294 art 2 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2022/0/78/#laws.0.1.0">2022 c 78 s 1</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.123B.20">
        <h1 class="shn">123B.20 DEALING IN SCHOOL SUPPLIES.</h1>
        <p>Except as provided for in sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/471.87">471.87</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/471.88">471.88</a>, no teacher in the public schools, nor any state, county, town, city, or district school officer, including any superintendent of schools, or any member of any school board, nor any person connected with the public school system in any capacity, shall be interested directly or indirectly in the sale, proceeds, or profits of any book, apparatus, or furniture used, or to be used, in any school with which the person is connected in any official capacity. Any person violating any of the provisions of this section shall forfeit not less than $50, nor more than $200 for each such offense. This section shall not apply to a person who may have an interest in the sale of any book of which that person is the author. Nothing in this section shall prohibit the spouse of an employee or officer covered by this section from contracting with the school district for the sale or lease of books, apparatus, furniture, or other supplies to be used in a school with which the employee or officer is connected in any official capacity, as long as the employee's or officer's position does not involve approving contracts for supplies and the school board unanimously approves the transaction.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.123B.20.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1959/1/71/#laws.8.15.0">Ex1959 c 71 art 8 s 15</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/121/#laws.0.1.0">1973 c 121 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/224/#laws.9.36.0">1993 c 224 art 9 s 36</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.6.124.0">1998 c 397 art 6 s 124</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.123B.21">
        <h1 class="shn">123B.21 DUTY OF OFFICERS TO REPORT VIOLATIONS OF LAW.</h1>
        <p>Every officer to whom reports are required by law to be made and for the failure to make which a penalty or fine or forfeiture is provided, shall give immediate written notice of such failure to the delinquent and to the proper county attorney. Such county attorney shall thereupon institute proper proceedings to collect such penalty, fine, or forfeiture. Upon complaint of the district superintendent, or when it comes to the attorney's knowledge that any school officer has violated any provision of law for which violation a penalty, fine or forfeiture is provided, such attorney shall institute like proceedings.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.123B.21.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1959/1/71/#laws.8.16.0">Ex1959 c 71 art 8 s 16</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/162/#laws.0.35.0">1975 c 162 s 35</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.6.124.0">1998 c 397 art 6 s 124</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.123B.22">
        <h1 class="shn">123B.22 COMBINATION TO CONTROL PRICES.</h1>
        <p>If at any time any publisher shall enter into any understanding, agreement, or combination to control the prices or to restrict competition in the adoption or sale of school books, each and every contract made by the publisher shall thereupon become null and void at the option of the other parties thereto.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.123B.22.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1959/1/71/#laws.8.21.0">Ex1959 c 71 art 8 s 21</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1980/0/609/#laws.6.32.0">1980 c 609 art 6 s 32</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.6.124.0">1998 c 397 art 6 s 124</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.123B.23">
        <h1 class="shn">123B.23 LIABILITY INSURANCE; OFFICERS AND EMPLOYEES.</h1>
        <p>The governing body of any independent school district may procure insurance against liability of the district or of its officers and employees for damages resulting from wrongful acts and omissions of the district and its officers and employees, whether the acts or omissions relate to governmental or proprietary functions of the district. Insofar as this insurance relates to governmental functions of the district, the policy of insurance shall contain a provision requiring the insurance company to waive the defense of governmental immunity up to the limits of the policy unless the district consents to the assertion of that defense.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.123B.23.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1959/1/71/#laws.4.23.0">Ex1959 c 71 art 4 s 23</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.6.101.0">1998 c 397 art 6 s 101</a>,124</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.123B.24">
        <h1 class="shn">123B.24 LEGAL ACTIONS BY DISTRICTS.</h1>
        <p>Any board may prosecute actions in the name of the district in the following cases:</p>
        <p>(1) On a contract made with the district, or with the board in its official capacity;</p>
        <p>(2) To enforce a liability, or a duty enjoined by law, in its favor or in favor of the district;</p>
        <p>(3) To recover a penalty or forfeiture given by law to it or to the district; or</p>
        <p>(4) To recover damages for an injury to the rights or property of the district.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.123B.24.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1959/1/71/#laws.8.2.0">Ex1959 c 71 art 8 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.6.120.0">1998 c 397 art 6 s 120</a>,124</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.123B.25">
        <h1 class="shn">123B.25 LEGAL ACTIONS AGAINST DISTRICTS AND TEACHERS.</h1>
        <p>(a) An action may be brought against any district, either upon a contract made with the district or its board, in its official capacity and within the scope of its authority, or for an injury to the rights of the plaintiff arising from some act or omission of such board. The action may be brought against the district even if the members of the board making the contract, or guilty of the act or omission complained of, are no longer in office.</p>
        <p>(b) Upon written request of the teacher involved, any district, however organized, must provide legal counsel for any school teacher against whom claim is made or action is brought for recovery of damages in any tort action involving physical injury to any person or property or for wrongful death arising out of or in connection with the employment of such teacher with the district. The choice of legal counsel shall be made only after consultation with the teacher. Provision of counsel under this paragraph shall not be construed to render the district liable for its torts, except as otherwise provided by law; or for reimbursement of costs of counsel provided to the teacher pursuant to the contract obligation of another or otherwise than under this paragraph; or for payment of any judgments or any other costs or disbursements in connection with a judgment where the judgment, cost or disbursement is against the teacher and not against the school district.</p>
        <p>(c) It is a defense to a civil action for damages against a school official, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/609.2231#stat.609.2231.5">609.2231, subdivision 5</a>, to prove that the force used by the official was reasonable, was in the exercise of lawful authority, and was necessary under the circumstances to restrain the pupil or to prevent bodily harm or death to another.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.123B.25.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1959/1/71/#laws.8.3.0">Ex1959 c 71 art 8 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1965/0/165/#laws.0.1.0">1965 c 165 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/162/#laws.0.41.0">1975 c 162 s 41</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/555/#laws.0.7.0">1990 c 555 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/647/#laws.9.12.0">1994 c 647 art 9 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.6.121.0">1998 c 397 art 6 s 121</a>,124</p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.123B.26"><b>123B.26</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/272/#laws.2.2.0">2014 c 272 art 2 s 2</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.123B.27"><b>123B.27</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/272/#laws.2.2.0">2014 c 272 art 2 s 2</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.123B.28">
        <h1 class="shn">123B.28 RECORDS AS EVIDENCE.</h1>
        <p>The records of all districts and boards and all transcripts thereof, or any part thereof, certified by the clerk or other officer having custody of the records or transcripts, shall be prima facie evidence of the facts stated in the records or transcripts. All records, books, and papers of the district or board shall be subject to the inspection of any voter of the district.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.123B.28.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1959/1/71/#laws.4.32.0">Ex1959 c 71 art 4 s 32</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.6.104.0">1998 c 397 art 6 s 104</a>,124</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.123B.29">
        <h1 class="shn">123B.29 SALE AT AUCTION.</h1>
        <p>Notwithstanding sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.52#stat.123B.52.1">123B.52, subdivision 1</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/471.345">471.345</a> or any other law, the board of a school district or of a cooperative center for vocational education may, in lieu of advertising for bids, sell at public auction to the highest responsible bidder a building constructed or to be constructed by a secondary or postsecondary school student or class as a school assignment. A board shall publish notice of a sale at least two weeks before the sale in the official newspaper of the district, or in the case of a cooperative center, in the official newspapers of each of the member districts, and may, at its discretion, publish additional notice in the official paper or elsewhere. A building may be withdrawn from sale prior to the completion of the sale unless the auction has been announced to be without reserve. If the sale is made at public auction, a duly licensed auctioneer must be retained to conduct the sale. The auctioneer shall be paid from the proceeds of the sale or from any funds available to the board which are not otherwise restricted or encumbered.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.123B.29.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1979/0/110/#laws.0.1.0">1979 c 110 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.6.105.0">1998 c 397 art 6 s 105</a>,124; art 11 s 3</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.123B.30">
        <h1 class="shn">123B.30 IMPROPER CLASSIFICATION OF PUPILS.</h1>
        <p>No district shall classify its pupils with reference to race, color, social position, or nationality, nor separate its pupils into different schools or departments upon any of such grounds. Any district so classifying or separating any of its pupils, or denying school privileges to any of its pupils upon any such ground shall forfeit its share in all apportioned school funds for any apportionment period in which such classification, separation, or exclusion shall occur or continue. The state commissioner upon notice to the offending district and upon proof of the violation of the provisions of this section, shall withhold in the semiannual apportionment the share of such district and the county auditor shall thereupon exclude such district from the apportionment for such period.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.123B.30.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1959/1/71/#laws.8.8.0">Ex1959 c 71 art 8 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.6.124.0">1998 c 397 art 6 s 124</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.123B.31">
        <h1 class="shn">123B.31 LIMITATION OF SECTIONS.</h1>
        <p>Material contained in sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/120A.22#stat.120A.22.1a">120A.22, subdivision 1a</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/120B.14">120B.14</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/120B.35">120B.35</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/121A.21">121A.21</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.44">122A.44</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.69">122A.69</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123A.22">123A.22</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123A.24">123A.24</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.02">123B.02</a>, subdivisions 1 to 15 and 17 to 20, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.09">123B.09</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.11">123B.11</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.14">123B.14</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.143">123B.143</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.147">123B.147</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.23">123B.23</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.49">123B.49</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.51">123B.51</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.52">123B.52</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.88">123B.88</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.02">124D.02</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.09">124D.09</a>, and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.51">124D.51</a>, unless expressly stated otherwise, relates only to independent school districts.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.123B.31.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1959/1/71/#laws.4.24.0">Ex1959 c 71 art 4 s 24</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1980/0/509/#laws.0.32.0">1980 c 509 s 32</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/209/#laws.2.1.0">1989 c 209 art 2 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.11.3.0">1998 c 397 art 11 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/254/#laws.0.10.0">2000 c 254 s 10</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/489/#laws.10.21.0">2000 c 489 art 10 s 21</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.123B.32">
        <h1 class="shn">123B.32 LANGUAGE ACCESS PLAN.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.32.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Language access plan required.</span></h2>
          <p>Starting in the 2025-2026 school year, during a regularly scheduled public board hearing, a school board must adopt a language access plan that specifies the district's process and procedures to render effective language assistance to students and adults who communicate in a language other than English. The language access plan must be available to the public and included in the school's handbook.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.32.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Plan requirements.</span></h2>
          <p>The language access plan must include how the district and its schools will use trained or certified spoken language interpreters for communication related to academic outcomes, progress, determinations, and placement of students in specialized programs and services; and how families and communities will be notified of their rights under this plan.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.32.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Regular review.</span></h2>
          <p>The board must review the plan every two years and update the plan as appropriate.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.123B.32.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/109/#laws.2.22.0">2024 c 109 art 2 s 22</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">PUBLIC SCHOOL FEES</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.123B.34">
        <h1 class="shn">123B.34 MINNESOTA PUBLIC SCHOOL FEE LAW, CITATION.</h1>
        <p>Sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.34">123B.34</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.39">123B.39</a> may be cited as the "Minnesota Public School Fee Law."</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.123B.34.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1974/0/561/#laws.0.1.0">1974 c 561 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.1.58.0">1998 c 397 art 1 s 58</a>; art 11 s 3</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.123B.35">
        <h1 class="shn">123B.35 GENERAL POLICY.</h1>
        <p>It is the policy of the state of Minnesota that public school education shall be free and no pupil shall be denied an education because of economic inability to furnish educational books and supplies necessary to complete educational requirements necessary for graduation. Any practice leading to suspension, coercion, exclusion, withholding of grades or diplomas, or discriminatory action based upon nonpayment of fees denies pupils their right to equal protection and entitled privileges. It is recognized that school boards do have the right to accept voluntary contributions, to make certain charges and to establish fees in areas considered extra curricular, noncurricular or supplementary to the requirements for the successful completion of a class or educational program, and to waive those fees under certain circumstances. No public school board may require, except as authorized by sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.36">123B.36</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.38">123B.38</a>, the payment of fees.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.123B.35.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1974/0/561/#laws.0.2.0">1974 c 561 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.1.58.0">1998 c 397 art 1 s 58</a>; art 11 s 3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/333/#laws.2.5.0">2010 c 333 art 2 s 5</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.123B.36">
        <h1 class="shn">123B.36 AUTHORIZED FEES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.36.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">School boards may require fees.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) For purposes of this subdivision, "home school" means a home school as defined in sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/120A.22">120A.22</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/120A.24">120A.24</a> with five or fewer students receiving instruction.</p>
          <p>(b) A school board is authorized to require payment of fees in the following areas:</p>
          <p>(1) in any program where the resultant product, in excess of minimum requirements and at the pupil's option, becomes the personal property of the pupil;</p>
          <p>(2) admission fees or charges for extracurricular activities, where attendance is optional and where the admission fees or charges a student must pay to attend or participate in an extracurricular activity is the same for all students, regardless of whether the student is enrolled in a public or a home school;</p>
          <p>(3) a security deposit for the return of materials, supplies, or equipment;</p>
          <p>(4) personal physical education and athletic equipment and apparel, although any pupil may personally provide it if it meets reasonable requirements and standards relating to health and safety established by the board;</p>
          <p>(5) items of personal use or products that a student has an option to purchase such as student publications, class rings, annuals, and graduation announcements;</p>
          <p>(6) fees specifically permitted by any other statute, including but not limited to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/171.05#stat.171.05.2">171.05, subdivision 2</a>; provided (i) driver education fees do not exceed the actual cost to the school and school district of providing driver education, and (ii) the driver education courses are open to enrollment to persons between the ages of 15 and 18 who reside or attend school in the school district;</p>
          <p>(7) field trips considered supplementary to a district educational program;</p>
          <p>(8) any authorized voluntary student health and accident benefit plan;</p>
          <p>(9) for the use of musical instruments owned or rented by the district, a reasonable rental fee not to exceed either the rental cost to the district or the annual depreciation plus the actual annual maintenance cost for each instrument;</p>
          <p>(10) transportation of pupils to and from extracurricular activities conducted at locations other than school, where attendance is optional;</p>
          <p>(11) transportation to and from school of pupils living within two miles from school and all other transportation services not required by law. If a district charges fees for transportation of pupils, it must establish guidelines for that transportation to ensure that no pupil is denied transportation solely because of inability to pay;</p>
          <p>(12) motorcycle classroom education courses conducted outside of regular school hours; provided the charge must not exceed the actual cost of these courses to the school district;</p>
          <p>(13) transportation to and from postsecondary institutions for pupils enrolled under the postsecondary enrollment options program under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.88#stat.123B.88.22">123B.88, subdivision 22</a>. Fees collected for this service must be reasonable and must be used to reduce the cost of operating the route. Families who qualify for mileage reimbursement under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.09#stat.124D.09.22">124D.09, subdivision 22</a>, may use their state mileage reimbursement to pay this fee. If no fee is charged, districts must allocate costs based on the number of pupils riding the route.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.36.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Students to furnish certain items.</span></h2>
          <p>Students may be required to furnish personal or consumable items including pencils, paper, pens, erasers and notebooks.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.36.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Students to provide for certain transportation.</span></h2>
          <p>Students may be required to furnish their own transportation to and from an instructional community-based employment station that is part of an approved occupational experience secondary vocational program. As an alternative, a board may require the payment of reasonable fees for transportation to and from these instructional community-based employment stations. This subdivision only applies to students who receive remuneration for their participation in these programs.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.36.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">School uniforms.</span></h2>
          <p>Notwithstanding section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.37">123B.37</a>, a board may require students to furnish or purchase clothing that constitutes a school uniform if the board has adopted a uniform requirement or program for the student's school. In adopting a uniform requirement, the board shall promote student, staff, parent, and community involvement in the program and account for the financial ability of students to purchase uniforms.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.36.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">School store permitted.</span></h2>
          <p>Sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.34">123B.34</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.39">123B.39</a> may not preclude the operation of a school store where pupils may purchase school supplies and materials.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.36.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Waiver of student fees.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A board may waive any deposit or fee for any pupil whose parent is serving in, or within the past year has served in, active military service as defined under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/190.05">190.05</a>.</p>
          <p>(b) A board may waive any deposit or fee if any pupil or the pupil's parent or guardian is unable to pay it.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.123B.36.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1974/0/561/#laws.0.3.0">1974 c 561 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1976/0/271/#laws.0.19.0">1976 c 271 s 19</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1978/0/764/#laws.0.7.0">1978 c 764 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/718/#laws.2.1.0">1988 c 718 art 2 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/562/#laws.2.1.0">1990 c 562 art 2 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/130/#laws.0.37.0">1991 c 130 s 37</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/499/#laws.12.29.0">1992 c 499 art 12 s 29</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/224/#laws.2.2.0">1993 c 224 art 2 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/226/#laws.3.5.0">1995 c 226 art 3 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/412/#laws.2.4.0">1996 c 412 art 2 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/388/#laws.0.1.0">1998 c 388 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.1.46.0">1998 c 397 art 1 s 46</a>-50,58; art 11 s 3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/398/#laws.5.5.0">1998 c 398 art 5 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/241/#laws.5.5.0">1999 c 241 art 5 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/6/#laws.1.6.0">1Sp2001 c 6 art 1 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/333/#laws.2.6.0">2010 c 333 art 2 s 6</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.123B.37">
        <h1 class="shn">123B.37 PROHIBITED FEES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.37.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Boards shall not charge certain fees.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A board is not authorized to charge fees in the following areas:</p>
          <p>(1) textbooks, workbooks, art materials, laboratory supplies, towels;</p>
          <p>(2) supplies necessary for participation in any instructional course except as authorized in sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.36">123B.36</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.38">123B.38</a>;</p>
          <p>(3) field trips that are required as a part of a basic education program or course;</p>
          <p>(4) graduation caps, gowns, any specific form of dress necessary for any educational program, and diplomas;</p>
          <p>(5) instructional costs for necessary school personnel employed in any course or educational program required for graduation;</p>
          <p>(6) library books required to be utilized for any educational course or program;</p>
          <p>(7) admission fees, dues, or fees for any activity the pupil is required to attend;</p>
          <p>(8) any admission or examination cost for any required educational course or program;</p>
          <p>(9) locker rentals;</p>
          <p>(10) transportation to and from school of pupils living two miles or more from school.</p>
          <p>(b) Notwithstanding paragraph (a), clauses (1) and (6), a board may charge fees for textbooks, workbooks, and library books, lost or destroyed by students. The board must annually notify parents or guardians and students about its policy to charge a fee under this paragraph.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.37.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Boards shall not withhold grades or diplomas for nonpayment of student fees.</span></h2>
          <p>No pupil's rights or privileges, including the receipt of grades or diplomas may be denied or abridged for nonpayment of fees; but this provision does not prohibit a district from maintaining any action provided by law for the collection of fees authorized by sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.36">123B.36</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.38">123B.38</a>. This provision applies to all Minnesota district school boards, charter school boards, and Tribal contract schools.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.123B.37.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1974/0/561/#laws.0.4.0">1974 c 561 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1976/0/271/#laws.0.20.0">1976 c 271 s 20</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/718/#laws.2.2.0">1988 c 718 art 2 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/130/#laws.0.37.0">1991 c 130 s 37</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/499/#laws.12.29.0">1992 c 499 art 12 s 29</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/3/#laws.9.5.0">1Sp1995 c 3 art 9 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/412/#laws.2.5.0">1996 c 412 art 2 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.1.51.0">1998 c 397 art 1 s 51</a>,58; art 11 s 3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/6/#laws.1.7.0">1Sp2001 c 6 art 1 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/109/#laws.2.23.0">2024 c 109 art 2 s 23</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.123B.38">
        <h1 class="shn">123B.38 HEARING.</h1>
        <p>Before the initiation of any fee not authorized or prohibited by sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.36">123B.36</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.37">123B.37</a>, the local board must hold a public hearing within the district upon three weeks published notice in the district's official newspaper, or such notice as is otherwise required for a regular board meeting given three weeks before the hearing on the proposed adoption of the policy.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.123B.38.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1974/0/561/#laws.0.5.0">1974 c 561 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1982/0/424/#laws.0.130.0">1982 c 424 s 130</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/0/248/#laws.0.70.0">1985 c 248 s 70</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/384/#laws.2.1.0">1987 c 384 art 2 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/422/#laws.0.10.0">1990 c 422 s 10</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/224/#laws.13.3.0">1993 c 224 art 13 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/465/#laws.3.64.0">1994 c 465 art 3 s 64</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/3/#laws.9.6.0">1Sp1995 c 3 art 9 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.1.52.0">1998 c 397 art 1 s 52</a>,58; art 11 s 3</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.123B.39">
        <h1 class="shn">123B.39 POSTSECONDARY INSTRUCTIONAL PROGRAMS.</h1>
        <p>Sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.34">123B.34</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.39">123B.39</a> may not be construed to prohibit a board from charging reasonable fees for goods and services provided in connection with any postsecondary instructional program, including but not limited to vocational technical, veteran farmer cooperative training, and community education programs, and continuing education and evening school programs other than those conducted pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.52">124D.52</a>.</p>
      </div>
