      <div class="history" id="stat.123B.39.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1974/0/561/#laws.0.6.0">1974 c 561 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/432/#laws.0.11.0">1975 c 432 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1980/0/609/#laws.4.22.0">1980 c 609 art 4 s 22</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.1.53.0">1998 c 397 art 1 s 53</a>,58; art 11 s 3</p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">AID TO NONPUBLIC STUDENTS</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.123B.40">
        <h1 class="shn">123B.40 DECLARATION OF POLICY.</h1>
        <p>It is the intent of the legislature to provide for distribution of educational aids such as textbooks, standardized tests and pupil support services so that every school pupil in the state will share equitably in education benefits and therefore further assure all Minnesota pupils and their parents freedom of choice in education.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.123B.40.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/396/#laws.0.1.0">1975 c 396 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1978/0/733/#laws.0.2.0">1978 c 733 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.7.164.0">1998 c 397 art 7 s 164</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.123B.41">
        <h1 class="shn">123B.41 DEFINITIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.41.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Application.</span></h2>
          <p>As used in sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.40">123B.40</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.48">123B.48</a>, the terms defined in this section shall have the meanings ascribed to them.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.41.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Textbook.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) "Textbook" means any book or book substitute, including electronic books as well as other printed materials delivered electronically, which a pupil uses as a text or text substitute in a particular class or program in the school regularly attended and a copy of which is expected to be available for the individual use of each pupil in this class or program. Textbook includes an online book with an annual subscription cost. Textbook includes a teacher's edition, teacher's guide, or other materials that accompany a textbook that a pupil uses when the teacher's edition, teacher's guide, or other teacher materials are packaged physically or electronically with textbooks for student use.</p>
          <p>(b) For purposes of calculating the annual nonpublic pupil aid entitlement for textbooks, the term shall be limited to books, workbooks, or manuals, whether bound or in loose-leaf form, as well as electronic books and other printed materials delivered electronically, intended for use as a principal source of study material for a given class or a group of students.</p>
          <p>(c) For purposes of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.40">123B.40</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.48">123B.48</a>, the terms "textbook" and "software or other educational technology" include only such secular, neutral, and nonideological materials as are available, used by, or of benefit to Minnesota public school pupils.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.41.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Standardized tests.</span></h2>
          <p>"Standardized tests" means standardized tests and scoring services which are provided by commercial publishing organizations or the state and which are in use in the public schools of Minnesota to measure the progress of pupils in secular subjects.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.41.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Pupil support services.</span></h2>
          <p>"Pupil support services" means guidance and counseling services and health services.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.41.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Individualized instructional or cooperative learning materials.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) "Individualized instructional or cooperative learning materials" means educational materials which:</p>
          <p>(1) are designed primarily for individual pupil use or use by pupils in a cooperative learning group in a particular class or program in the school the pupil regularly attends, including teacher materials that accompany materials that a pupil uses;</p>
          <p>(2) are secular, neutral, nonideological and not capable of diversion for religious use; and</p>
          <p>(3) are available, used by, or of benefit to Minnesota public school pupils.</p>
          <p>(b) Subject to the requirements in paragraph (a), "individualized instructional or cooperative learning materials" include, but are not limited to, the following if they do not fall within the definition of "textbook" in subdivision 2: published materials; periodicals; documents; pamphlets; photographs; reproductions; pictorial or graphic works; prerecorded video programs; prerecorded tapes, cassettes and other sound recordings; manipulative materials; desk charts; games; study prints and pictures; desk maps; models; learning kits; blocks or cubes; flash cards; individualized multimedia systems; prepared instructional computer software programs; choral and band sheet music; electronic books and other printed materials delivered electronically; and CD-Rom.</p>
          <p>(c) "Individualized instructional or cooperative learning materials" do not include instructional equipment, instructional hardware, or ordinary daily consumable classroom supplies.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.41.5a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5a.<span class="headnote">Software or other educational technology.</span></h2>
          <p>For purposes of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.42">123B.42 </a>and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.43">123B.43</a>, "software or other educational technology" includes software, programs, applications, hardware, and any other electronic educational technology. Software or other educational technology includes course registration fees for advanced placement courses delivered online.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.41.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Pupils.</span></h2>
          <p>"Pupils" means elementary and secondary pupils.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.41.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Elementary pupils.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">"Elementary pupils" means pupils in grades kindergarten through 6; provided, each kindergarten pupil in a half-day program shall be counted as one-half pupil for all computations pursuant to sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.40">123B.40</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.42">123B.42</a>, and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.44">123B.44</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.48">123B.48</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.41.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Secondary pupils.</span></h2>
          <p>"Secondary pupils" means pupils in grades 7 through 12.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.41.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Nonpublic school defined.</span></h2>
          <p>"Nonpublic school" means any school, church or religious organization, or home school wherein a resident of Minnesota may legally fulfill the compulsory instruction requirements of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/120A.22">120A.22</a>, which is located within the state, and which meets the requirements of Title VI of the Civil Rights Act of 1964 (Public Law 88-352). It does not mean a public school.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.41.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">Nonsectarian nonpublic school.</span></h2>
          <p>"Nonsectarian nonpublic school" means any nonpublic school as defined in subdivision 9, which is not church related, is not controlled by a church, and does not promote a religious belief.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.41.11">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 11.<span class="headnote">Pupil; student.</span></h2>
          <p>"Pupil" or "student" means a child enrolled in a school and is limited to children who are residents, or children of residents, of Minnesota.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.41.12">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 12.<span class="headnote">Intermediary service area.</span></h2>
          <p>"Intermediary service area" means a school administrative unit approved by the commissioner, other than a single school district, including but not limited to the following:</p>
          <p>(a) a service cooperative;</p>
          <p>(b) a cooperative of two or more school districts;</p>
          <p>(c) learning centers; or</p>
          <p>(d) an association of schools or school districts.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.41.13">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 13.<span class="headnote">Neutral site.</span></h2>
          <p>"Neutral site" means a public center, a nonsectarian nonpublic school, a mobile unit located off the nonpublic school premises, or any other location off the nonpublic school premises which is neither physically nor educationally identified with the functions of the nonpublic school.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.41.14">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 14.<span class="headnote">Guidance and counseling services.</span></h2>
          <p>"Guidance and counseling services" means all activities of a licensed counselor in counseling pupils and parents, providing counseling on learning problems, evaluating the abilities of pupils, assisting pupils in personal and social development and providing referral assistance.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.41.15">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 15.<span class="headnote">Health services.</span></h2>
          <p>"Health services" means physician, dental, nursing or optometric services and health supplies brought to the site by the health professional for pupil usage in the field of physical or mental health; provided the term does not include direct educational instruction, services which are required pursuant to sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/125A.03">125A.03</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/125A.24">125A.24</a>, and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/125A.65">125A.65</a>, and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/125A.26">125A.26</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/125A.48">125A.48</a>, or services which are eligible to receive special education aid pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/125A.75">125A.75</a>.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.123B.41.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/396/#laws.0.2.0">1975 c 396 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1978/0/733/#laws.0.3.0">1978 c 733 s 3</a>-13; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1979/0/34/#laws.0.1.0">1979 c 34 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1980/0/609/#laws.4.2.0">1980 c 609 art 4 s 2</a>; art 6 s 20,21; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1982/0/424/#laws.0.32.0">1982 c 424 s 32</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/209/#laws.2.1.0">1989 c 209 art 2 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/130/#laws.0.7.0">1991 c 130 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/224/#laws.13.29.0">1993 c 224 art 13 s 29</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/647/#laws.13.4.0">1994 c 647 art 13 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/186/#laws.0.119.0">1995 c 186 s 119</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/305/#laws.1.138.0">1996 c 305 art 1 s 138</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/412/#laws.8.1.0">1996 c 412 art 8 s 1</a>-4; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.7.10.0">1998 c 397 art 7 s 10</a>,164; art 11 s 3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/1/11/#laws.1.9.0">1Sp2011 c 11 art 1 s 9</a>,10; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/144/#laws.0.1.0">2012 c 144 s 1</a>,2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/116/#laws.1.6.0">2013 c 116 art 1 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/1/5/#laws.1.5.0">1Sp2017 c 5 art 1 s 5</a>,6; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/11/#laws.1.2.0">1Sp2019 c 11 art 1 s 2</a>,3</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.123B.42">
        <h1 class="shn">123B.42 TEXTBOOKS; INDIVIDUAL INSTRUCTION OR COOPERATIVE LEARNING MATERIAL; STANDARD TESTS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.42.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Providing education materials and tests.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of education shall promulgate rules under the provisions of chapter 14 requiring that in each school year, based upon formal requests by or on behalf of nonpublic school pupils in a nonpublic school, the local districts or intermediary service areas must purchase or otherwise acquire textbooks, individualized instructional or cooperative learning materials, software or other educational technology, and standardized tests and loan or provide them for use by children enrolled in that nonpublic school. These textbooks, individualized instructional or cooperative learning materials, software or other educational technology, and standardized tests must be loaned or provided free to the children for the school year for which requested. The loan or provision of the textbooks, individualized instructional or cooperative learning materials, and standardized tests shall be subject to rules prescribed by the commissioner of education.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.42.1a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 1a.<span class="headnote">Curriculum; electronic components.</span></h2>
          <p>A school district that provides curriculum to resident students that has both physical and electronic components must make the electronic component accessible to a resident student in a home school in compliance with sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/120A.22">120A.22</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/120A.24">120A.24</a> at the request of the student or the student's parent or guardian, provided that the district does not incur more than an incidental cost as a result of providing access electronically.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.42.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Title to education materials and tests.</span></h2>
          <p>The title to textbooks, individualized instructional or cooperative learning materials, software or other educational technology, and standardized testing materials must remain in the servicing school district or intermediary service area, and possession or custody may be granted or charged to administrators of the nonpublic school attended by the nonpublic school pupil or pupils to whom the textbooks, individualized instructional or cooperative learning materials, or standardized tests are loaned or provided.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.42.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Cost; limitation.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The cost per pupil of the textbooks, individualized instructional or cooperative learning materials, software or other educational technology, and standardized tests provided for in this section for each school year must not exceed the statewide average expenditure per pupil, adjusted pursuant to paragraph (b), by the Minnesota public elementary and secondary schools for textbooks, individualized instructional materials and standardized tests as computed and established by the department by February 1 of the preceding school year from the most recent public school year data then available.</p>
          <p>(b) The cost computed in paragraph (a) shall be increased by an inflation adjustment equal to the percent of increase in the formula allowance, pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/126C.10#stat.126C.10.2">126C.10, subdivision 2</a>, from the second preceding school year to the current school year.</p>
          <p>(c) The commissioner shall allot to the districts or intermediary service areas the total cost for each school year of providing or loaning the textbooks, individualized instructional or cooperative learning materials, software or other educational technology, and standardized tests for the pupils in each nonpublic school. The allotment shall not exceed the product of the statewide average expenditure per pupil, according to paragraph (a), adjusted pursuant to paragraph (b), multiplied by the number of nonpublic school pupils who make requests pursuant to this section and who are enrolled as of September 15 of the current school year.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.123B.42.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/396/#laws.0.3.0">1975 c 396 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1978/0/733/#laws.0.14.0">1978 c 733 s 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1980/0/609/#laws.4.3.0">1980 c 609 art 4 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1982/0/424/#laws.0.130.0">1982 c 424 s 130</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1982/0/642/#laws.0.10.0">1982 c 642 s 10</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/314/#laws.1.22.0">1983 c 314 art 1 s 22</a>; art 6 s 9; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/486/#laws.0.18.0">1988 c 486 s 18</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/3/#laws.16.13.0">1Sp1995 c 3 art 16 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/412/#laws.8.5.0">1996 c 412 art 8 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.7.11.0">1998 c 397 art 7 s 11</a>,164; art 11 s 3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/398/#laws.5.55.0">1998 c 398 art 5 s 55</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/5/#laws.2.1.0">1Sp2001 c 5 art 2 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/6/#laws.1.8.0">1Sp2001 c 6 art 1 s 8</a>,55 subd 2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/130/#laws.0.12.0">2003 c 130 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/5/#laws.4.1.0">1Sp2005 c 5 art 4 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/144/#laws.0.3.0">2012 c 144 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/116/#laws.1.7.0">2013 c 116 art 1 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/0/50/#laws.1.32.0">2019 c 50 art 1 s 32</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/11/#laws.1.4.0">1Sp2019 c 11 art 1 s 4</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.123B.43">
        <h1 class="shn">123B.43 USE OF INDIVIDUALIZED INSTRUCTIONAL MATERIALS.</h1>
        <p>(a) The commissioner shall assure that textbooks and individualized instructional materials loaned to nonpublic school pupils are secular, neutral, nonideological and that they are incapable of diversion for religious use.</p>
        <p>(b) Textbooks, individualized instructional materials, software or other educational technology must not be used in religious courses, devotional exercises, religious training or any other religious activity.</p>
        <p>(c) Textbooks and individualized instructional materials must be loaned only to individual pupils upon the request of a parent or guardian or the pupil on a form designated for this use by the commissioner. The request forms shall provide for verification by the parent or guardian or pupil that the requested textbooks and individualized instructional materials are for the use of the individual pupil in connection with a program of instruction in the pupil's elementary or secondary school.</p>
        <p>(d) The servicing school district or the intermediary service area must take adequate measures to ensure an accurate and periodic inventory of all textbooks, individualized instructional materials, software or other educational technology loaned to elementary and secondary school pupils attending nonpublic schools. The commissioner of education shall promulgate rules under the provisions of chapter 14 to terminate the eligibility of any nonpublic school pupil if the commissioner determines, after notice and opportunity for hearing, that the textbooks, individualized instructional materials, or software or other educational technology, have been used in a manner contrary to the provisions of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.41#stat.123B.41.5">123B.41, subdivision 5</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.42">123B.42</a>, or this section or any rules promulgated by the commissioner of education.</p>
        <p>(e) Nothing contained in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.41#stat.123B.41.5">123B.41, subdivision 5</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.42">123B.42</a>, or this section shall be construed to authorize the making of any payments to a nonpublic school or its faculty, staff or administrators for religious worship or instruction or for any other purpose.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.123B.43.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1980/0/609/#laws.4.5.0">1980 c 609 art 4 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1982/0/424/#laws.0.130.0">1982 c 424 s 130</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/562/#laws.7.6.0">1990 c 562 art 7 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/224/#laws.13.30.0">1993 c 224 art 13 s 30</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.7.20.0">1998 c 397 art 7 s 20</a>,164; art 11 s 3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/398/#laws.5.55.0">1998 c 398 art 5 s 55</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/130/#laws.0.12.0">2003 c 130 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/144/#laws.0.4.0">2012 c 144 s 4</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.123B.44">
        <h1 class="shn">123B.44 PROVISION OF PUPIL SUPPORT SERVICES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.44.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Provided services.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of education shall promulgate rules under the provisions of chapter 14 requiring each district or other intermediary service area: (a) to provide each year upon formal request by a specific date by or on behalf of a nonpublic school pupil enrolled in a nonpublic school located in that district or area, the same specific health services as are provided for public school pupils by the district where the nonpublic school is located; and (b) to provide each year upon formal request by a specific date by or on behalf of a nonpublic school secondary pupil enrolled in a nonpublic school located in that district or area, the same specific guidance and counseling services as are provided for public school secondary pupils by the district where the nonpublic school is located. The district where the nonpublic school is located must provide the necessary transportation within the district boundaries between the nonpublic school and a public school or neutral site for nonpublic school pupils who are provided pupil support services under this section if the district elects to provide pupil support services at a site other than the nonpublic school. Each request for pupil support services must set forth the guidance and counseling or health services requested by or on behalf of all eligible nonpublic school pupils enrolled in a given nonpublic school. No district or intermediary service area must not expend an amount for these pupil support services which exceeds the amount allotted to it under this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.44.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Location of services.</span></h2>
          <p>Health and guidance and counseling services may be provided to nonpublic school pupils under this section at a public school, a neutral site, the nonpublic school or any other suitable location. District or intermediary service area personnel and representatives of the nonpublic school pupils receiving pupil support services must hold an annual consultation regarding the type of services, provider of services, and the location of the provision of these services. The district board or intermediary service area governing board must make the final decision on the location of the provision of these services.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.44.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Guidance and counseling; exclusions.</span></h2>
          <p>Guidance and counseling services provided to nonpublic school pupils pursuant to this section shall not include the planning or selection of particular courses or classroom activities of the nonpublic school.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.44.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Health services; allotment.</span></h2>
          <p>Each school year the commissioner shall allot to the school districts or other intermediary service areas for the provision of health services pursuant to this section the actual cost of the services provided for the pupils in each respective nonpublic school for that school year. The allotment must not exceed the average expenditure per public school pupil for these services by those Minnesota public elementary and secondary schools which provide health services to public school pupils, multiplied by the number of pupils in that particular nonpublic school who request these health services and who are enrolled as of September 15 of the current school year.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.44.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Guidance and counseling services; allotment.</span></h2>
          <p>Each school year the commissioner shall allot to the school districts or intermediary service areas for the provision of guidance and counseling services pursuant to this section the actual cost of the services provided for the pupils in each respective nonpublic school for that school year. The allotment for guidance and counseling services for the secondary pupils in each nonpublic school must not exceed the average expenditure per public school secondary pupil for these services by those Minnesota public schools which provide these services to their secondary pupils, multiplied by the number of secondary pupils in that particular nonpublic school who request these services and who are enrolled as of September 15 of the current school year.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.44.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Computation of maximum allotments.</span></h2>
          <p>For purposes of computing maximum allotments for each school year pursuant to this section, the average public school expenditure per pupil for health services and the average public school expenditure per secondary pupil for guidance and counseling services shall be computed and established by the department by February 1 of the preceding school year from the most recent public school year data then available.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.123B.44.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/396/#laws.0.5.0">1975 c 396 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1978/0/733/#laws.0.15.0">1978 c 733 s 15</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1982/0/424/#laws.0.130.0">1982 c 424 s 130</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/1/12/#laws.7.17.0">1Sp1985 c 12 art 7 s 17</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/178/#laws.0.6.0">1987 c 178 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/629/#laws.0.27.0">1988 c 629 s 27</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/224/#laws.9.27.0">1993 c 224 art 9 s 27</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/3/#laws.16.13.0">1Sp1995 c 3 art 16 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/412/#laws.8.6.0">1996 c 412 art 8 s 6</a>,7; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.7.12.0">1998 c 397 art 7 s 12</a>-16,164; art 11 s 3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/398/#laws.2.22.0">1998 c 398 art 2 s 22</a>,23; art 5 s 55; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/6/#laws.1.9.0">1Sp2001 c 6 art 1 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/130/#laws.0.12.0">2003 c 130 s 12</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.123B.445">
        <h1 class="shn">123B.445 NONPUBLIC EDUCATION COUNCIL.</h1>
        <p>(a) The commissioner shall appoint a 15-member Council on Nonpublic Education. The 15 members shall represent various areas of the state, represent various methods of providing nonpublic education, and shall be knowledgeable about nonpublic education. The compensation, removal of members, filling of vacancies, and terms are governed by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/15.0575">15.0575</a>. The council shall not expire. The council shall advise the commissioner on issues affecting nonpublic education and nonpublic schools. The council may recognize educational accrediting agencies, for the sole purpose of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/120A.22">120A.22</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/120A.24">120A.24</a>, and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/120A.26">120A.26</a>.</p>
        <p>(b) A parent or guardian of a nonpublic school pupil or a nonpublic school may file a complaint about services provided under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.40">123B.40</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.42">123B.42</a>, and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.44">123B.44</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.48">123B.48</a> with the Nonpublic Education Council. The council may review the complaint and make a recommendation for resolution to the commissioner.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.123B.445.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/396/#laws.0.5.0">1975 c 396 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1978/0/733/#laws.0.15.0">1978 c 733 s 15</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1982/0/424/#laws.0.130.0">1982 c 424 s 130</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/1/12/#laws.7.17.0">1Sp1985 c 12 art 7 s 17</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/178/#laws.0.6.0">1987 c 178 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/629/#laws.0.27.0">1988 c 629 s 27</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/224/#laws.9.27.0">1993 c 224 art 9 s 27</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/3/#laws.16.13.0">1Sp1995 c 3 art 16 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/412/#laws.8.6.0">1996 c 412 art 8 s 6</a>,7; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.7.12.0">1998 c 397 art 7 s 12</a>-16,164; art 11 s 3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/398/#laws.2.22.0">1998 c 398 art 2 s 22</a>,23; art 5 s 55</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.123B.45">
        <h1 class="shn">123B.45 PAYMENTS FOR CONTRACTUAL OBLIGATIONS.</h1>
        <p>The commissioner shall make such payments to school districts or intermediary service areas pursuant to sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.40">123B.40</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.42">123B.42</a>, and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.44">123B.44</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.48">123B.48</a> as are needed to meet contractual obligations incurred for the provision of benefits to nonpublic school students pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.42">123B.42</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.44">123B.44</a>, or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.445">123B.445</a>.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.123B.45.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/396/#laws.0.6.0">1975 c 396 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1978/0/733/#laws.0.16.0">1978 c 733 s 16</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.7.17.0">1998 c 397 art 7 s 17</a>,164; art 11 s 3</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.123B.46">
        <h1 class="shn">123B.46 ADMINISTRATIVE COSTS.</h1>
        <p>Each year, a district or intermediary service area may claim and receive from the department an additional sum for the administration of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.42">123B.42</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.44">123B.44</a>, and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.445">123B.445</a>, equal to five percent of the district's or area's allocation for that year pursuant to those sections.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.123B.46.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1978/0/733/#laws.0.17.0">1978 c 733 s 17</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/562/#laws.7.5.0">1990 c 562 art 7 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/3/#laws.16.13.0">1Sp1995 c 3 art 16 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.7.18.0">1998 c 397 art 7 s 18</a>,164; art 11 s 3</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.123B.47">
        <h1 class="shn">123B.47 NOTICE TO DISTRICTS; PRORATION.</h1>
        <p>If the appropriation for nonpublic educational aid under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.40">123B.40</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.48">123B.48</a> is not sufficient to meet the required payments in any fiscal year, the department must notify the school districts at the earliest possible date of the need to prorate the appropriation among the districts.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.123B.47.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/398/#laws.6.1.0">1987 c 398 art 6 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/3/#laws.16.13.0">1Sp1995 c 3 art 16 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.7.19.0">1998 c 397 art 7 s 19</a>,164; art 11 s 3</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.123B.48">
        <h1 class="shn">123B.48 LIMIT ON DISTRICT OBLIGATIONS.</h1>
        <p>If the amount appropriated for purposes of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.40">123B.40</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.42">123B.42</a>, and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.44">123B.44</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.48">123B.48</a>, for any year is not sufficient to make the payments required pursuant to sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.40">123B.40</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.42">123B.42</a>, and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.44">123B.44</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.48">123B.48</a>, for that year, then no school district or intermediary service area is required to expend an amount pursuant to sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.40">123B.40</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.42">123B.42</a>, and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.44">123B.44</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.48">123B.48</a>, for that year which exceeds the amount of the payments it receives pursuant to sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.40">123B.40</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.42">123B.42</a>, and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.44">123B.44</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.48">123B.48</a>, for that year.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.123B.48.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/396/#laws.0.8.0">1975 c 396 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1978/0/733/#laws.0.18.0">1978 c 733 s 18</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1979/0/334/#laws.6.18.0">1979 c 334 art 6 s 18</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1980/0/609/#laws.4.4.0">1980 c 609 art 4 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/358/#laws.6.17.0">1981 c 358 art 6 s 17</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.7.164.0">1998 c 397 art 7 s 164</a>; art 11 s 3</p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">COCURRICULAR AND EXTRACURRICULAR ACTIVITIES</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.123B.49">
        <h1 class="shn">123B.49 EXTRACURRICULAR ACTIVITIES; INSURANCE.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.49.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Activities outside district limits.</span></h2>
          <p>Whenever it appears to be beneficial and for the best interest of the district and the pupils of the district to carry on any school sport activities or educational activities connected with their studies outside of the territorial limits of the district, the board may authorize such activities to be conducted under such rules and regulations as the board deems sufficient. The district may pay all necessary costs therefor including transportation from the district funds available.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.49.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Cocurricular activities authorization.</span></h2>
          <p>The board must take charge of and control all cocurricular school activities of the teachers and children of the public schools in that district held in the school building or school grounds or under the supervision or direction of the school board. The board must adopt rules and regulations for the conduct of cocurricular activities in which the schools of the district or any class or pupils therein may participate. All money received on account of such activities must be turned over to the district treasurer, who shall keep the same in the general fund, to be disbursed for expenses and salaries connected with the activities, or otherwise, by the board upon properly allowed itemized claims.</p>
          <p>Teachers or pupils in the public schools of such district must not participate in cocurricular activities, nor shall the school name or any allied name be used in connection therewith, except by consent and direction of the board.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.49.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Cocurricular activities.</span></h2>
          <p>Cocurricular activities means school sponsored and directed activities designed to provide opportunities for pupils to participate, on an individual or group basis, in school and public events for the improvement of skills. Cocurricular activities are not offered for school credit, cannot be counted toward graduation and have one or more of the following characteristics:</p>
          <p>(a) They are conducted at regular and uniform times during school hours, or at times established by school authorities;</p>
          <p>(b) Although not offered for credit, they are directed or supervised by instructional staff in a learning environment similar to that found in courses offered for credit;</p>
          <p>(c) They are partially funded by public moneys for general instructional purposes under direction and control of the board.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.49.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Board control of extracurricular activities.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The board must take charge of and control all extracurricular activities of the teachers and children of the public schools in the district. Extracurricular activities means all direct and personal services for pupils for their enjoyment that are managed and operated under the guidance of an adult or staff member. The board shall allow all resident pupils receiving instruction in a home school as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.36#stat.123B.36.1">123B.36, subdivision 1</a>, paragraph (a), to be eligible to fully participate in extracurricular activities on the same basis as public school students.</p>
          <p>(b) Extracurricular activities have all of the following characteristics:</p>
          <p>(1) they are not offered for school credit nor required for graduation;</p>
          <p>(2) they are generally conducted outside school hours, or if partly during school hours, at times agreed by the participants, and approved by school authorities;</p>
          <p>(3) the content of the activities is determined primarily by the pupil participants under the guidance of a staff member or other adult.</p>
          <p>(c) Any or all costs of these activities may be provided from school revenues and all revenues and expenditures for these activities shall be recorded in the same manner as other revenues and expenditures of the district.</p>
          <p>(d) The teachers or pupils in the district must not participate in such activity, nor shall the school name or any allied name be used in connection therewith, except by consent and direction of the board.</p>
          <p>(e) A school district must reserve revenue raised for extracurricular activities and spend the revenue only for extracurricular activities.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.49.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Contract for insurance.</span></h2>
          <p>The board may enter into a contract providing for the payment of cash benefits or the rendering or payment of hospital and medical benefits, or both to school children injured while participating in activities of the school. The contract entered into by the board may make the payment of such benefits or the rendering thereof the direct and sole obligation of the association or company entering into such contract with the district.</p>
          <p>If the board deems it advisable, it may authorize employees to collect fees from the pupils enrolled in said school who are to be or are covered by such contract, and to make payment of the premium or other charge for such contract or protection. The payment of such premium or other charge may be made from funds received from the federal government or from the state or any governmental subdivision thereof, or from funds derived by a tax levy or the issuance of bonds.</p>
          <p>The child's payment of any fees, premium or other charge shall not thereby make the district liable for any injuries incurred from such school activities.</p>
          <p>The commissioner of education may purchase medical insurance coverage for the benefit of students of the Minnesota State Academy for the Deaf or the Minnesota State Academy for the Blind in the same manner and with the same effect as a school district board may do for its students under this subdivision.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.49.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Insurance laws.</span></h2>
          <p>The insurance laws of this state shall not apply to nonprofit benefit and relief associations formed by public schools or officers of public schools or the Minnesota State High School League, the privileges of which and applications for membership in which are confined to pupils of the schools, and the benefits and relief to be derived therefrom are limited to pupils injured or disabled from participation in school athletics or any supervised school activity.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.123B.49.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1959/1/71/#laws.4.20.0">Ex1959 c 71 art 4 s 20</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1971/0/254/#laws.0.1.0">1971 c 254 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1976/0/212/#laws.0.1.0">1976 c 212 s 1</a>-5; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1979/0/335/#laws.0.14.0">1979 c 335 s 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/258/#laws.0.12.0">1987 c 258 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/384/#laws.1.55.0">1987 c 384 art 1 s 55</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/246/#laws.0.2.0">1989 c 246 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/562/#laws.8.25.0">1990 c 562 art 8 s 25</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/265/#laws.9.40.0">1991 c 265 art 9 s 40</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/412/#laws.13.14.0">1996 c 412 art 13 s 14</a>,15; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.6.78.0">1998 c 397 art 6 s 78</a>-82,124; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/398/#laws.5.55.0">1998 c 398 art 5 s 55</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/241/#laws.5.6.0">1999 c 241 art 5 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/130/#laws.0.12.0">2003 c 130 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/5/#laws.2.55.0">1Sp2005 c 5 art 2 s 55</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/11/#laws.1.5.0">1Sp2019 c 11 art 1 s 5</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.123B.492">
        <h1 class="shn">123B.492 SUPERVISED COMPETITIVE HIGH SCHOOL DIVING.</h1>
        <p>Notwithstanding Minnesota Rules, part <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/4717.3750">4717.3750</a>, any pool built before January 1, 1987, that was used for a one-meter board high school diving program during the 2000-2001 school year may be used for supervised competitive one-meter board high school diving. Schools and school districts are strongly encouraged to use a pool for supervised competitive high school diving that meets the requirements of Minnesota Rules, part <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/4717.3750">4717.3750</a>. A school or district using a pool for supervised competitive high school diving for either training practice or competition that does not meet the requirements of Minnesota Rules, part <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/4717.3750">4717.3750</a>, must provide appropriate notice to parents and participants as to the type of variance from Minnesota Rules and risk it may present.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.123B.492.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/329/#laws.9.16.0">1989 c 329 art 9 s 16</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/425/#laws.0.2.0">1990 c 425 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/9/#laws.2.40.0">1Sp2003 c 9 art 2 s 40</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/288/#laws.6.10.0">2004 c 288 art 6 s 10</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/294/#laws.2.15.0">2004 c 294 art 2 s 15</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/5/#laws.4.2.0">1Sp2005 c 5 art 4 s 2</a></p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">SPECIAL SCHOOL DISTRICTS;<br>MINNEAPOLIS AND SOUTH ST. PAUL</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.123B.50">
        <h1 class="shn">123B.50 SPECIAL SCHOOL DISTRICTS, LAWS APPLICABLE.</h1>
        <p>Special districts as now organized shall continue to operate under the special legislation and charter provisions governing them until conversion to independent districts. The provisions of law relating to independent districts shall apply to and govern each special district unless the special laws and charter provisions governing the special district provide for the matter, in which case the special laws and charter provisions relating to the special district shall apply and control. Sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/205A.01">205A.01</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/205A.11">205A.11</a> control and supersede inconsistent provisions of special laws or charters in the administration of school district elections in special districts.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.123B.50.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1959/1/71/#laws.4.25.0">Ex1959 c 71 art 4 s 25</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1980/0/609/#laws.6.19.0">1980 c 609 art 6 s 19</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/266/#laws.2.11.0">1987 c 266 art 2 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.6.124.0">1998 c 397 art 6 s 124</a></p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">SCHOOL BUILDINGS AND OTHER FACILITIES;<br>EQUIPMENT; CONSTRUCTION; LAND AND SITES</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.123B.51">
        <h1 class="shn">123B.51 SCHOOLHOUSES AND SITES; USES FOR SCHOOL AND NONSCHOOL PURPOSES; CLOSINGS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.51.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Sites.</span></h2>
          <p>According to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/126C.40#stat.126C.40.1">126C.40, subdivision 1</a>, or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/465.71">465.71</a>, when funds are available, the board may locate and acquire necessary sites of schoolhouses or enlargements, or additions to existing schoolhouse sites by lease, purchase or condemnation under the power of eminent domain; it may erect schoolhouses on the sites; it may erect or purchase garages for district-owned school buses. When property is taken by eminent domain by authority of this subdivision when needed by the district for such purposes, the fact that the property has been acquired by the owner under the power of eminent domain or is already devoted to public use, shall not prevent its acquisition by the district. The board may sell or exchange schoolhouses or sites, and execute deeds of conveyance thereof.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.51.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Use of schoolhouses.</span></h2>
          <p>The board may authorize the use of any schoolhouses in the district for divine worship, Sunday schools, public meetings, elections, postsecondary instruction, and other community purposes that, in its judgment, will not interfere with their use for school purposes. Before permitting any of these uses, the board may require a cash or corporate surety bond in a reasonable amount conditioned for the proper use of the schoolhouse, payment of all rent, and repair of all damage caused by the use. It may determine a reasonable charge for using the schoolhouse.</p>
          <p>It may authorize the use of any schoolhouses or buildings owned or leased by the district for primaries, elections, registrations, and related activities if the board determines that the use will not interfere with school purposes. It may impose reasonable regulations and conditions upon the use as may seem necessary and proper.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.51.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Lease real property.</span></h2>
          <p>When necessary, the board may lease real property for school purposes.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.51.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Lease for nonschool purpose.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The board may lease to any person, business, or organization real property that is not needed for school purposes, or part of the property that is not needed for school purposes if the board determines that leasing part of the property does not interfere with the educational programs taking place on the property. The board may charge and collect reasonable consideration for the lease and may determine the terms and conditions of the lease.</p>
          <p>(b) In districts with outstanding bonds, the net proceeds of the lease must be first deposited in the debt retirement fund of the district in an amount sufficient to meet when due that percentage of the principal and interest payments for outstanding bonds that is ascribable to the payment of expenses necessary and incidental to the construction or purchase of the particular building or property that is leased. Any remaining net proceeds in these districts may be deposited in either the debt redemption fund or operating capital account. All net proceeds of the lease in districts without outstanding bonds shall be deposited in the operating capital account of the district.</p>
          <p>(c) The board may make capital improvements to the real property, not exceeding in cost the replacement value of the property, to facilitate its rental, and the lease of the improved property, or part of it, shall provide for rentals which will recover the cost of the improvements over the initial term of the lease. Notwithstanding paragraph (b), the portion of the rentals representing the cost of the improvements shall be deposited in the operating capital account of the district and the balance of the rentals shall be used as provided in paragraph (b).</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.51.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Schoolhouse closing.</span></h2>
          <p>The board may close a schoolhouse only after a public hearing on the question of the necessity and practicability of the proposed closing. Published notice of the hearing shall be given for two weeks in the official newspaper of the district. The time and place of the meeting, the description and location of the schoolhouse, and a statement of the reasons for the closing must be specified in the notice. Parties requesting to give testimony for and against the proposal shall be heard by the board before it makes a final decision to close or not to close the schoolhouse.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.51.5a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5a.<span class="headnote">Temporary closing.</span></h2>
          <p>A school district that proposes to temporarily close a schoolhouse or that intends to lease the facility to another entity for use as a schoolhouse for three or fewer years is not subject to subdivision 5 if the school board holds a public meeting and allows public comment on the schoolhouse's future.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.51.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Proceeds of sale or exchange.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Proceeds of the sale or exchange of school buildings or real property of the district must be used as provided in this subdivision.</p>
          <p>(b) In districts with outstanding bonds, the proceeds of the sale or exchange shall first be deposited in the debt retirement fund of the district in an amount sufficient to meet when due that percentage of the principal and interest payments for outstanding bonds which is ascribable to the payment of expenses necessary and incidental to the construction or purchase of the particular building or property which is sold.</p>
          <p>(c) After satisfying the requirements of paragraph (b), a district with outstanding bonds may deposit proceeds of the sale or exchange in its general fund reserved for operating capital account if the amount deposited is used for the following:</p>
          <p>(1) for expenditures for the cleanup of polychlorinated biphenyls, if the method for cleanup is approved by the department;</p>
          <p>(2) for capital expenditures for the betterment, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/475.51#stat.475.51.8">475.51, subdivision 8</a>, of district-owned school buildings; or</p>
          <p>(3) to replace the building or property sold.</p>
          <p>(d) In a district with outstanding bonds, the amount of the proceeds of the sale or exchange remaining after the application of paragraphs (b) and (c), which is sufficient to meet when due that percentage of the principal and interest payments for the district's outstanding bonds which is not governed by paragraph (b), shall be deposited in the debt retirement fund.</p>
          <p>(e) Any proceeds of the sale or exchange remaining in districts with outstanding bonds after the application of paragraphs (b), (c), and (d), and all proceeds of the sale or exchange in districts without outstanding bonds shall be deposited in the general fund reserved for operating capital account of the district.</p>
          <p>(f) Notwithstanding paragraphs (c) and (d), a district with outstanding bonds may deposit in its general fund reserved for operating capital account and use for any lawful operating capital expenditure without the reduction of any levy limitation the same percentage of the proceeds of the sale or exchange of a building or property as the percentage of the initial cost of purchasing or constructing the building or property which was paid using revenue from the general fund reserved for operating capital account.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.51.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Use of buildings by lower grades.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) In addition to the protections provided in existing building and fire code rules and standards, the following alternatives apply for existing school buildings:</p>
          <p>(1) rooms occupied by preschool, kindergarten, and first and second grade students for classrooms, latchkey, day care, early childhood family education or teen parent or similar programs may be located on any floor level below the fourth story of a school building if the building is protected throughout by a complete automatic sprinkler system and a complete automatic fire alarm system consisting of automatic smoke detection throughout the exit system and approved smoke detection in all rooms and areas other than classrooms and offices;</p>
          <p>(2) rooms used by preschool, kindergarten, or first grade students for classrooms, latchkey, day care, early childhood family education or teen parent or similar programs, must be located on the story of exit discharge, and rooms used by second grade students, for any purpose, must be located on the story of exit discharge or one story above unless one of the following conditions is met:</p>
          <p>(i) a complete automatic sprinkler system is provided throughout the building, the use of the affected room or space is limited to one grade level at a time, and exiting is provided from the affected room or space which is independent from the exiting system used by older students; or</p>
          <p>(ii) a complete approved automatic fire alarm system is installed throughout the building consisting of automatic smoke detection throughout the exit system and approved detection in all rooms and areas other than classrooms and offices, the use of the affected room or space is limited to one grade level at a time and exiting is provided from the affected room or space which is independent from the exiting system used by older students.</p>
          <p>(b) For purposes of paragraph (a), clause (2), pupils from second grade down are considered one grade level.</p>
          <p>(c) Accessory spaces, including gymnasiums, cafeterias, media centers, auditoriums, libraries, and band and choir rooms, which are used on an occasional basis by preschool, kindergarten, and first and second grade students are permitted to be located one level above or one level below the story of exit discharge, provided the building is protected throughout by a complete automatic sprinkler system or a complete approved corridor smoke detection system.</p>
          <p>(d) Paragraphs (a) and (c) supersede any contrary provisions of the State Fire Code or State Building Code and rules relating to those codes must be amended by the state agencies having jurisdiction of them.</p>
          <p>(e) Paragraphs (a) to (d) are effective for new school buildings beginning July 1, 1994.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.123B.51.history
			">

      <div class="section" id="stat.123B.52">
        <h1 class="shn">123B.52 CONTRACTS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.52.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Contracts.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">A contract for work or labor, or for the purchase of furniture, fixtures, or other property, except books registered under the copyright laws and information systems software, or for the construction or repair of school houses, the estimated cost or value of which shall exceed that specified in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/471.345#stat.471.345.3">471.345, subdivision 3</a>, must not be made by the school board without first advertising for bids or proposals by two weeks' published notice in the official newspaper. This notice must state the time and place of receiving bids and contain a brief description of the subject matter.</p>
          <p class="in">Additional publication in the official newspaper or elsewhere may be made as the board shall deem necessary.</p>
          <p class="in">After taking into consideration conformity with the specifications, terms of delivery, and other conditions imposed in the call for bids, every such contract for which a call for bids has been issued must be awarded to the lowest responsible bidder, be duly executed in writing, and be otherwise conditioned as required by law. The person to whom the contract is awarded shall give a sufficient bond to the board for its faithful performance. Notwithstanding section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/574.26">574.26</a> or any other law to the contrary, on a contract limited to the purchase of a finished tangible product, a board may require, at its discretion, a performance bond of a contractor in the amount the board considers necessary. A record must be kept of all bids, with names of bidders and amount of bids, and with the successful bid indicated thereon. A bid containing an alteration or erasure of any price contained in the bid which is used in determining the lowest responsible bid must be rejected unless the alteration or erasure is corrected as provided in this section. An alteration or erasure may be crossed out and the correction thereof printed in ink or typewritten adjacent thereto and initialed in ink by the person signing the bid. In the case of identical low bids from two or more bidders, the board may, at its discretion, utilize negotiated procurement methods with the tied low bidders for that particular transaction, so long as the price paid does not exceed the low tied bid price. In the case where only a single bid is received, the board may, at its discretion, negotiate a mutually agreeable contract with the bidder so long as the price paid does not exceed the original bid. If no satisfactory bid is received, the board may readvertise. Standard requirement price contracts established for supplies or services to be purchased by the district must be established by competitive bids. Such standard requirement price contracts may contain escalation clauses and may provide for a negotiated price increase or decrease based upon a demonstrable industrywide or regional increase or decrease in the vendor's costs. Either party to the contract may request that the other party demonstrate such increase or decrease. The term of such contracts must not exceed two years with an option on the part of the district to renew for an additional two years, except as provided in subdivision 3 or 7. Contracts for the purchase of perishable food items, except milk for school lunches and vocational training programs, in any amount may be made by direct negotiation by obtaining two or more written quotations for the purchase or sale, when possible, without advertising for bids or otherwise complying with the requirements of this section or section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/471.345#stat.471.345.3">471.345, subdivision 3</a>. All quotations obtained shall be kept on file for a period of at least one year after receipt.</p>
          <p class="in">Every contract made without compliance with the provisions of this section shall be void. Except in the case of the destruction of buildings or injury thereto, where the public interest would suffer by delay, contracts for repairs may be made without advertising for bids.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.52.1a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 1a.<span class="headnote">Construction contracts.</span></h2>
          <p>A project labor agreement is a hiring agreement that establishes wages, uniform work schedules, and rules for dispute resolution to manage construction projects that generally require, among other things, payment of union dues or fees to a labor organization or membership in or affiliation with a labor organization. A school board must adopt at a public meeting a written resolution authorizing a project labor agreement to construct or repair a facility through a contract or bid. The board must publish in the official newspaper of the district notice of the meeting at least 30 days in advance.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.52.1b">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 1b.<span class="headnote">Best value alternative.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">As an alternative to the procurement method described in subdivision 1, a contract for construction, building, alteration, improvement, or repair work may be awarded to the vendor or contractor offering the best value under a request for proposals as described in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16C.28#stat.16C.28.1">16C.28, subdivision 1</a>, paragraph (a), clause (2), and paragraph (c).</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.52.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Contract within budgeted amounts.</span></h2>
          <p>The board may authorize its superintendent or business manager to lease, purchase, and contract for goods and services within the budget as approved by the board. Any transaction in an amount exceeding the minimum amount for which bids are required must first be specifically authorized by the board and must fulfill all other applicable requirements in subdivision 1.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.52.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Transportation; fuel.</span></h2>
          <p>Notwithstanding the provisions of subdivision 1 or section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/471.345">471.345</a>, a contract for the transportation of school children, or a contract for the purchase of petroleum heating fuel or fuel for vehicles may be made by direct negotiation, by obtaining two or more written quotations for the service when possible, or upon sealed bids. At least 30 days before awarding a directly negotiated contract, the district must, by published notice, request quotations for the service to be provided. All quotations obtained must be kept on file for a period of at least one year after receipt. If a contract is made by direct negotiation, all quotations must be public information. If a contract is made upon sealed bids, the procedure for advertising and awarding bids shall conform to the provisions of subdivision 1 except as otherwise provided in this subdivision. The term of such contracts must not exceed ten years.</p>
          <p>Notwithstanding the provisions of subdivision 1 or section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/574.26">574.26</a>, a performance bond must be required of a contractor on a contract for the transportation of school children only when deemed necessary by and at the discretion of the board. Such a performance bond must be in the amount determined by the board.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.52.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Asbestos removal and polychlorinated biphenyls cleanup.</span></h2>
          <p>Notwithstanding any law to the contrary, districts may, without an election, enter into contracts extending beyond the end of the fiscal year to pay the costs of removal or encapsulation of asbestos or cleanup of polychlorinated biphenyls found in school buildings or on school property.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.52.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Contracts with board members.</span></h2>
          <p>Members of the board are authorized to contract with, to work for, and furnish supplies to the district subject to the provisions of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/471.87">471.87</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.52.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Disposing of surplus school computers.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Notwithstanding section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/471.345">471.345</a>, governing school district contracts made upon sealed bid or otherwise complying with the requirements for competitive bidding, other provisions of this section governing school district contracts, or other law to the contrary, a school district under this subdivision may dispose of school computers, including a tablet device.</p>
          <p>(b) A school district may dispose of a surplus school computer and related equipment if the district disposes of the surplus property by conveying the property and title to:</p>
          <p>(1) another school district;</p>
          <p>(2) the state Department of Corrections;</p>
          <p>(3) the Board of Trustees of the Minnesota State Colleges and Universities;</p>
          <p>(4) the family of a student residing in the district whose total family income meets the federal definition of poverty; or</p>
          <p>(5) a charitable organization under section 501(c)(3) of the Internal Revenue Code that is registered with the attorney general's office for educational use.</p>
          <p>(c) If surplus school computers are not disposed of under paragraph (b), upon adoption of a written resolution of the school board, when updating or replacing school computers, including tablet devices, used primarily by students, a school district may sell or give used computers or tablets to qualifying students at the price specified in the written resolution. A student is eligible to apply to the school board for a computer or tablet under this subdivision if the student is currently enrolled in the school and intends to enroll in the school in the year following the receipt of the computer or tablet. If more students apply for computers or tablets than are available, the school must first qualify students whose families are eligible for free or reduced-price meals, and then dispose of the remaining computers or tablets by lottery.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.52.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Food service contracts.</span></h2>
          <p>A contract between a school board and a food service management company that complies with Code of Federal Regulations, title 7, section 210.16, may be renewed annually after its initial term for not more than four additional years.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.123B.52.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1959/1/71/#laws.4.18.0">Ex1959 c 71 art 4 s 18</a>,19; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1967/1/1/#laws.0.6.0">Ex1967 c 1 s 6</a>; art 6 s 5,6; art 7 s 20; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/463/#laws.7.9.0">1984 c 463 art 7 s 9</a>,10; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/0/279/#laws.0.1.0">1985 c 279 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/1/12/#laws.7.14.0">1Sp1985 c 12 art 7 s 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/258/#laws.0.6.0">1987 c 258 s 6</a>;
