art 8 s 23,24; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/130/#laws.0.37.0">1991 c 130 s 37</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/499/#laws.12.29.0">1992 c 499 art 12 s 29</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/224/#laws.5.2.0">1993 c 224 art 5 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/3/#laws.16.13.0">1Sp1995 c 3 art 16 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/412/#laws.13.13.0">1996 c 412 art 13 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.6.69.0">1998 c 397 art 6 s 69</a>-77,124; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/489/#laws.5.3.0">2000 c 489 art 5 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/9/#laws.4.3.0">1Sp2003 c 9 art 4 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/148/#laws.3.12.0">2007 c 148 art 3 s 12</a>,13; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/189/#laws.30.1.0">2016 c 189 art 30 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/1/5/#laws.6.1.0">1Sp2017 c 5 art 6 s 1</a>,2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/11/#laws.6.3.0">1Sp2019 c 11 art 6 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2020/1/8/#laws.1.1.0">1Sp2020 c 8 art 1 s 1</a>,2</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.123B.53">
        <h1 class="shn">123B.53 DEBT SERVICE EQUALIZATION PROGRAM.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.53.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) For purposes of this section, the eligible debt service revenue of a district is defined as follows:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) the amount needed to produce between five and six percent in excess of the amount needed to meet when due the principal and interest payments on the obligations of the district for eligible projects according to subdivision 2, excluding the amounts listed in paragraph (b), minus</p>
          <p class="in">(2) the amount of debt service excess levy reduction for that school year calculated according to the procedure established by the commissioner.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) The obligations in this paragraph are excluded from eligible debt service revenue:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) obligations under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.61">123B.61</a>;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) the part of debt service principal and interest paid from the taconite environmental protection fund or Douglas J. Johnson economic protection trust, excluding the portion of taconite payments from the Iron Range schools and community development account under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/298.28#stat.298.28.7a">298.28, subdivision 7a</a>;</p>
          <p class="in">(3) obligations for long-term facilities maintenance under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.595">123B.595</a>;</p>
          <p class="in">(4) obligations under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.62">123B.62</a>; and</p>
          <p class="in">(5) obligations equalized under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.535">123B.535</a>.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) For purposes of this section, if a preexisting school district reorganized under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123A.35">123A.35</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123A.43">123A.43</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123A.46">123A.46</a>, and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123A.48">123A.48</a> is solely responsible for retirement of the preexisting district's bonded indebtedness or capital loans, debt service equalization aid must be computed separately for each of the preexisting districts.</p>
          <p class="in">(d) For purposes of this section, the adjusted net tax capacity determined according to sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/127A.48">127A.48</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/273.1325">273.1325</a> shall be adjusted to include the tax capacity of property generally exempted from ad valorem taxes under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/272.02">272.02</a>, subdivision 64.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.53.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Eligibility.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The following portions of a district's debt service levy qualify for debt service equalization:</p>
          <p>(1) debt service for repayment of principal and interest on bonds issued before July 2, 1992;</p>
          <p>(2) debt service for bonds refinanced after July 1, 1992, if the bond schedule has been approved by the commissioner and, if necessary, adjusted to reflect a 20-year maturity schedule; and</p>
          <p>(3) debt service for bonds issued after July 1, 1992, for construction projects that have received a positive review and comment according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.71">123B.71</a>, if the commissioner has determined that the district has met the criteria under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/126C.69#stat.126C.69.3">126C.69, subdivision 3</a>, and if the bond schedule has been approved by the commissioner and, if necessary, adjusted to reflect a 20-year maturity schedule.</p>
          <p>(b) The criterion described in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/126C.69#stat.126C.69.3">126C.69, subdivision 3</a>, paragraph (a), clause (9), does not apply to bonds authorized by elections held before July 1, 1992.</p>
          <p>(c) For the purpose of this subdivision the department shall determine the eligibility for sparsity at the location of the new facility, or the site of the new facility closest to the nearest operating school if there is more than one new facility.</p>
          <p>(d) Notwithstanding paragraphs (a) to (c), debt service for repayment of principal and interest on bonds issued after July 1, 1997, does not qualify for debt service equalization aid unless the primary purpose of the facility is to serve students in kindergarten through grade 12.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.53.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Notification.</span></h2>
          <p>A district eligible for debt service equalization revenue under subdivision 2 must notify the commissioner of the amount of its intended debt service revenue calculated under subdivision 1 for all bonds sold prior to the notification by July 1 of the calendar year the levy is certified.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.53.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Debt service equalization revenue.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) The debt service equalization revenue of a district equals the sum of the first tier debt service equalization revenue and the second tier debt service equalization revenue.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) The first tier debt service equalization revenue of a district equals the greater of zero or the eligible debt service revenue minus the amount raised by a levy of 15.74 percent times the adjusted net tax capacity of the district minus the second tier debt service equalization revenue of the district.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) The second tier debt service equalization revenue of a district equals the greater of zero or the eligible debt service revenue, minus the amount raised by a levy of 26.24 percent times the adjusted net tax capacity of the district.</p>
          <p class="in">(d) Notwithstanding paragraphs (b) and (c), for a district with a capital loan under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/126C.60">126C.60</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/126C.72">126C.72</a>, the first tier debt equalization revenue equals zero, and the second tier debt equalization revenue equals the portion of the district's eligible debt service levy under subdivision 2 in excess of the district's maximum effort debt service levy under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/126C.63#stat.126C.63.8">126C.63, subdivision 8</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.53.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Equalized debt service levy.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The equalized debt service levy of a district equals the sum of the first tier equalized debt service levy and the second tier equalized debt service levy.</p>
          <p>(b) A district's first tier equalized debt service levy equals the district's first tier debt service equalization revenue times the lesser of one or the ratio of:</p>
          <p>(1) the quotient derived by dividing the adjusted net tax capacity of the district for the year before the year the levy is certified by the adjusted pupil units in the district for the school year ending in the year prior to the year the levy is certified; to</p>
          <p>(2) $3,400 in fiscal year 2016, $4,430 in fiscal year 2017, and the greater of $4,430 or 55.33 percent of the initial equalizing factor in fiscal year 2018 and later.</p>
          <p>(c) A district's second tier equalized debt service levy equals the district's second tier debt service equalization revenue times the lesser of one or the ratio of:</p>
          <p>(1) the quotient derived by dividing the adjusted net tax capacity of the district for the year before the year the levy is certified by the adjusted pupil units in the district for the school year ending in the year prior to the year the levy is certified; to</p>
          <p>(2) $8,000 in fiscal years 2016 and 2017, and the greater of $8,000 or 100 percent of the initial equalizing factor in fiscal year 2018 and later.</p>
          <p>(d) For the purposes of this subdivision, the initial equalizing factor equals the quotient derived by dividing the total adjusted net tax capacity of all school districts in the state for the year before the year the levy is certified by the total number of adjusted pupil units in all school districts in the state in the year before the year the levy is certified.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.53.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Debt service equalization aid.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A district's debt service equalization aid is the sum of the district's first tier debt service equalization aid and the district's second tier debt service equalization aid.</p>
          <p>(b) A district's first tier debt service equalization aid equals the difference between the district's first tier debt service equalization revenue and the district's first tier equalized debt service levy.</p>
          <p>(c) A district's second tier debt service equalization aid equals the difference between the district's second tier debt service equalization revenue and the district's second tier equalized debt service levy.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.53.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Debt service equalization aid payment schedule.</span></h2>
          <p>Debt service equalization aid must be paid according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/127A.45#stat.127A.45.10">127A.45, subdivision 10</a>.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.123B.53.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/265/#laws.5.8.0">1991 c 265 art 5 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/499/#laws.5.14.0">1992 c 499 art 5 s 14</a>-19;       <div class="section" id="stat.123B.535">
        <h1 class="shn">123B.535 NATURAL DISASTER DEBT SERVICE EQUALIZATION.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.535.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) For purposes of this section, the eligible natural disaster debt service revenue of a district is defined as the amount needed to produce between five and six percent in excess of the amount needed to meet when due the principal and interest payments on the obligations of the district that would otherwise qualify under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.53">123B.53</a> under the following conditions:</p>
          <p>(1) the district was impacted by a natural disaster event or area occurring January 1, 2005, or later, as declared by the President of the United States of America, which is eligible for Federal Emergency Management Agency payments;</p>
          <p>(2) the natural disaster caused $500,000 or more in damages to school district buildings; and</p>
          <p>(3) the repair and replacement costs are not covered by insurance payments or Federal Emergency Management Agency payments.</p>
          <p>(b) For purposes of this section, the adjusted net tax capacity equalizing factor equals the quotient derived by dividing the total adjusted net tax capacity of all school districts in the state for the year before the year the levy is certified by the total number of adjusted pupil units in the state for the year prior to the year the levy is certified.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) For purposes of this section, the adjusted net tax capacity determined according to sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/127A.48">127A.48</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/273.1325">273.1325</a> shall be adjusted to include the tax capacity of property generally exempted from ad valorem taxes under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/272.02#stat.272.02.64">272.02, subdivision 64</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.535.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Notification.</span></h2>
          <p>A district eligible for natural disaster debt service equalization revenue under subdivision 1 must notify the commissioner of the amount of its intended natural disaster debt service revenue calculated under subdivision 1 for all bonds sold prior to the notification by July 1 of the calendar year the levy is certified.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.535.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Natural disaster debt service equalization revenue.</span></h2>
          <p>The debt service equalization revenue of a district equals the greater of zero or the eligible debt service revenue, minus the greater of zero or the difference between:</p>
          <p>(1) the amount raised by a levy of ten percent times the adjusted net tax capacity of the district; and</p>
          <p>(2) the district's eligible debt service revenue under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.53">123B.53</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.535.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Equalized natural disaster debt service levy.</span></h2>
          <p>A district's equalized natural disaster debt service levy equals the district's natural disaster debt service equalization revenue times the lesser of one or the ratio of:</p>
          <p>(1) the quotient derived by dividing the adjusted net tax capacity of the district for the year before the year the levy is certified by the adjusted pupil units in the district for the school year ending in the year prior to the year the levy is certified; to</p>
          <p>(2) 300 percent of the statewide adjusted net tax capacity equalizing factor.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.535.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Natural disaster debt service equalization aid.</span></h2>
          <p>A district's natural disaster debt service equalization aid equals the difference between the district's natural disaster debt service equalization revenue and the district's equalized natural disaster debt service levy.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.535.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Natural disaster debt service equalization aid payment schedule.</span></h2>
          <p>Debt service equalization aid must be paid according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/127A.45#stat.127A.45.10">127A.45, subdivision 10</a>.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.123B.535.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/312/#laws.18.5.0">2014 c 312 art 18 s 5</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.123B.54">
        <h1 class="shn">123B.54 DEBT SERVICE APPROPRIATION.</h1>
        <p class="in">(a) The amount necessary to make debt service equalization aid payments under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.53">123B.53 </a>and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.535">123B.535 </a>is annually appropriated from the general fund to the commissioner of education.</p>
        <p class="in">(b) The appropriations in paragraph (a) must be reduced by the amount of any money specifically appropriated for the same purpose in any year from any state fund.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.123B.54.history
			">

      <div class="section" id="stat.123B.55">
        <h1 class="shn">123B.55 DEBT SERVICE LEVY.</h1>
        <p>A district may levy the amounts necessary to make payments for bonds issued and for interest on them, including the bonds and interest on them, issued as authorized by Minnesota Statutes 1974, section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/275.125#stat.275.125.3">275.125, subdivision 3</a>, clause (7)(C); and the amounts necessary for repayment of debt service loans and capital loans, minus the amount of debt service equalization revenue of the district.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.123B.55.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/265/#laws.5.10.0">1991 c 265 art 5 s 10</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.7.124.0">1998 c 397 art 7 s 124</a>,164</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.123B.56">
        <h1 class="shn">123B.56 HEALTH, SAFETY, AND ENVIRONMENTAL MANAGEMENT.</h1>
        <p>"Health, safety, and environmental management" means school district activities necessary for a district's compliance with state law and rules of the Departments of Health, Labor and Industry, Public Safety, and Pollution Control Agency as well as any related federal standards. These activities include hazard assessment, required training, record keeping, and program management.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.123B.56.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/224/#laws.5.21.0">1993 c 224 art 5 s 21</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.7.164.0">1998 c 397 art 7 s 164</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.123B.57">
        <h1 class="shn">123B.57 HEALTH AND SAFETY PROJECTS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.57.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.</h2>
          <p> [Repealed by amendment, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/1/3/#laws.6.3.0">1Sp2015 c 3 art 6 s 3</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.57.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.</h2>
          <p> [Repealed by amendment, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/1/3/#laws.6.3.0">1Sp2015 c 3 art 6 s 3</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.57.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.</h2>
          <p> [Repealed by amendment, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/1/3/#laws.6.3.0">1Sp2015 c 3 art 6 s 3</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.57.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.</h2>
          <p> [Repealed by amendment, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/1/3/#laws.6.3.0">1Sp2015 c 3 art 6 s 3</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.57.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.</h2>
          <p> [Repealed by amendment, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/1/3/#laws.6.3.0">1Sp2015 c 3 art 6 s 3</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.57.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote"> Health and safety capital projects.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Health and safety capital projects may include expenditures necessary for the correction of fire and life safety hazards; design, purchase, installation, maintenance, and inspection of fire protection and alarm equipment; purchase or construction of appropriate facilities for the storage of combustible and flammable materials; inventories and facility modifications not related to a remodeling project to comply with lab safety requirements under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/121A.31">121A.31</a>; inspection, testing, repair, removal or encapsulation, and disposal of asbestos-containing building materials; cleanup and disposal of polychlorinated biphenyls; cleanup and disposal of hazardous and infectious wastes; cleanup, removal, disposal, and repairs related to storing heating fuel or transportation fuels such as alcohol, gasoline, fuel oil, and special fuel, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/296A.01">296A.01</a>; correction of occupational safety and health administration regulated hazards; indoor air quality inspections, investigations, and testing; mold abatement; upgrades or replacement of mechanical ventilation systems to meet American Society of Heating, Refrigerating and Air Conditioning Engineers standards and State Mechanical Code; design, materials, and installation of local exhaust ventilation systems, including required make-up air for controlling regulated hazardous substances; correction of Department of Health Food Code violations; correction of swimming pool hazards excluding depth correction; playground safety inspections, repair of unsafe outdoor playground equipment, and the installation of impact surfacing materials; bleacher repair or rebuilding to comply with the order of a building code inspector under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/326B.112">326B.112</a>; testing and mitigation of elevated radon hazards; lead testing; copper in water testing; cleanup after major weather-related disasters or flooding; reduction of excessive organic and inorganic levels in wells and capping of abandoned wells; installation and testing of boiler backflow valves to prevent contamination of potable water; vaccinations, titers, and preventative supplies for bloodborne pathogen compliance; costs to comply with the Janet B. Johnson Parents' Right to Know Act; automated external defibrillators and other emergency plan equipment and supplies specific to the district's emergency action plan; compliance with the National Emission Standards for Hazardous Air Pollutants for school generators established by the United States Environmental Protection Agency; and health, safety, and environmental management costs associated with implementing the district's health and safety program including costs to establish and operate safety committees, in school buildings or property owned or being acquired by the district. Testing and calibration activities are permitted for existing mechanical ventilation systems at intervals no less than every five years.</p>
          <p>(b) For fiscal years 2014 through 2017, a school district must not include expenses related to emission compliance projects for school generators in its health and safety capital projects unless it reduces its approved spending on other qualified health and safety projects by the same amount.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.57.6a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6a.</h2>
          <p> [Repealed by amendment, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/1/3/#laws.6.3.0">1Sp2015 c 3 art 6 s 3</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.57.6b">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6b.</h2>
          <p> [Repealed by amendment, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/1/3/#laws.6.3.0">1Sp2015 c 3 art 6 s 3</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.57.6c">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6c.</h2>
          <p> [Repealed by amendment, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/1/3/#laws.6.3.0">1Sp2015 c 3 art 6 s 3</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.57.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.</h2>
          <p> [Repealed by amendment, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/1/3/#laws.6.3.0">1Sp2015 c 3 art 6 s 3</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.57.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Health, safety, and environmental management.</span></h2>
          <p> The department may contract with regional service organizations, private contractors, Minnesota Safety Council, or state agencies to provide management assistance to school districts for health and safety capital projects. Management assistance is the development of written programs for the identification, recognition and control of hazards, and prioritization and scheduling of district health and safety capital projects. The commissioner shall not mandate management assistance or exclude private contractors from the opportunity to provide any health and safety services to school districts.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.123B.57.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/718/#laws.8.19.0">1988 c 718 art 8 s 19</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/719/#laws.5.84.0">1988 c 719 art 5 s 84</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/329/#laws.5.11.0">1989 c 329 art 5 s 11</a>-13; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/1/1/#laws.6.6.0">1Sp1989 c 1 art 6 s 6</a>; art 9 s 4; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/562/#laws.5.8.0">1990 c 562 art 5 s 8</a>; art 10 s 5; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/604/#laws.8.1.0">1990 c 604 art 8 s 1</a>,2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/130/#laws.0.19.0">1991 c 130 s 19</a>,20; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/265/#laws.5.6.0">1991 c 265 art 5 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/224/#laws.5.22.0">1993 c 224 art 5 s 22</a>-26; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/647/#laws.6.28.0">1994 c 647 art 6 s 28</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/3/#laws.5.7.0">1Sp1995 c 3 art 5 s 7</a>; art 16 s 13; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/1/4/#laws.4.17.0">1Sp1997 c 4 art 4 s 17</a>,18; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/299/#laws.0.30.0">1998 c 299 s 30</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.7.97.0">1998 c 397 art 7 s 97</a>,98,164; art 11 s 3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/398/#laws.4.3.0">1998 c 398 art 4 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/86/#laws.1.33.0">1999 c 86 art 1 s 33</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/241/#laws.4.7.0">1999 c 241 art 4 s 7</a>,29; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/464/#laws.3.9.0">2000 c 464 art 3 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/489/#laws.5.5.0">2000 c 489 art 5 s 5</a>,19,27; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/6/#laws.4.3.0">1Sp2001 c 6 art 4 s 3</a>-5; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/9/#laws.4.5.0">1Sp2003 c 9 art 4 s 5</a>-7; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2006/0/282/#laws.5.1.0">2006 c 282 art 5 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/146/#laws.4.7.0">2007 c 146 art 4 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/96/#laws.4.2.0">2009 c 96 art 4 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/1/11/#laws.4.2.0">1Sp2011 c 11 art 4 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/292/#laws.1.4.0">2012 c 292 art 1 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/116/#laws.6.3.0">2013 c 116 art 6 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/312/#laws.18.7.0">2014 c 312 art 18 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/1/3/#laws.6.3.0">1Sp2015 c 3 art 6 s 3</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.123B.571">
        <h1 class="shn">123B.571 RADON TESTING.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.571.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Voluntary plan.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">The commissioners of health and education may jointly develop a plan to encourage school districts to accurately and efficiently test for the presence of radon in public school buildings serving students in kindergarten through grade 12. To the extent possible, the commissioners shall base the plan on the standards established by the United States Environmental Protection Agency.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.571.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Radon testing.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">A school district may include radon testing as a part of its ten-year facility plan under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.595#stat.123B.595.4">123B.595, subdivision 4</a>. If a school district receives authority to use long-term facilities maintenance revenue to conduct radon testing, the district shall conduct the testing according to the radon testing plan developed by the commissioners of health and education.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.571.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Reporting.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">A school district that has tested its school buildings for the presence of radon shall report the results of its tests to the Department of Health in a form and manner prescribed by the commissioner of health. A school district that has tested for the presence of radon shall also report the results of its testing at a school board meeting.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.123B.571.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/224/#laws.5.44.0">1993 c 224 art 5 s 44</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/189/#laws.30.4.0">2016 c 189 art 30 s 4</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.123B.572">
        <h1 class="shn">123B.572 SOLAR PANEL FIRE SAFETY.</h1>
        <p>A solar photovoltaic system installed at a school must comply with chapter 690 of the most current edition of NFPA 70, the National Electrical Code, adopted under the authority given in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/326B.32#stat.326B.32.2">326B.32, subdivision 2</a>.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.123B.572.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/189/#laws.30.5.0">2016 c 189 art 30 s 5</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.123B.58">
        <h1 class="shn">123B.58 DISABILITY ACCESS AND FIRE SAFETY IMPROVEMENTS TO SCHOOL BUILDINGS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.58.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Removal of architectural barriers.</span></h2>
          <p>If a board has insufficient money in its capital expenditure fund to remove architectural barriers from a building it owns in order to allow a pupil to attend a school in the pupil's attendance area or to meet the needs of an employee with a disability, a district may submit an application to the commissioner containing at least the following:</p>
          <p>(1) program modifications that the board considered, such as relocating classrooms, providing an accessible unisex bathroom, providing alternative library resources, or using special equipment, such as bookcarts, and the reasons the modifications were not feasible;</p>
          <p>(2) a description of the proposed building modifications and the cost of the modifications; and</p>
          <p>(3) the age and market value of the building.</p>
          <p>Individuals developing an application for a district shall complete a workshop, developed jointly by the commissioner and the Council on Disability, about access criteria.</p>
          <p>In consultation with the Council on Disability, the commissioner shall develop criteria to determine the cost-effectiveness of removing barriers in older buildings.</p>
          <p>The commissioner shall approve or disapprove an application within 60 days of receiving it.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.58.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Fire safety modifications.</span></h2>
          <p>If a district has insufficient money in its capital expenditure fund to make modifications to a school building required by a fire inspection conducted according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/299F.47">299F.47</a>, the district may submit an application to the commissioner containing information required by the commissioner. The commissioner shall approve or disapprove of the application according to criteria established by the commissioner. The criteria shall take into consideration the cost-effectiveness of making modifications to older buildings.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.58.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Levy authority.</span></h2>
          <p>The district may levy up to $300,000 under this section, as approved by the commissioner. The approved amount may be levied over eight or fewer years.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.58.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Levy authority in combined districts.</span></h2>
          <p>Notwithstanding subdivision 3, a district that has combined or consolidated may levy up to 50 percent times $300,000 times the number of former districts that operated on June 30, 1991, in the area that now makes up the combined or consolidated district. The approved amount is reduced by any amount levied under subdivision 3 in the consolidated or combined district or in the former districts that make up the consolidated or combined district. Levy authority under this subdivision expires at the same time as levy authority under subdivision 3.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.123B.58.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/265/#laws.5.7.0">1991 c 265 art 5 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/499/#laws.5.13.0">1992 c 499 art 5 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/647/#laws.5.5.0">1994 c 647 art 5 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/3/#laws.5.8.0">1Sp1995 c 3 art 5 s 8</a>; art 16 s 13; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.7.99.0">1998 c 397 art 7 s 99</a>,100,164; art 11 s 3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/241/#laws.4.29.0">1999 c 241 art 4 s 29</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/464/#laws.3.7.0">2000 c 464 art 3 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/489/#laws.5.19.0">2000 c 489 art 5 s 19</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/294/#laws.5.6.0">2004 c 294 art 5 s 6</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.123B.59"><b>123B.59 </b>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/1/3/#laws.6.14.0">1Sp2015 c 3 art 6 s 14</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.123B.591"><b>123B.591 </b>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/1/3/#laws.6.14.0">1Sp2015 c 3 art 6 s 14</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.123B.595">
        <h1 class="shn">123B.595 LONG-TERM FACILITIES MAINTENANCE REVENUE.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.595.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Long-term facilities maintenance revenue.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Long-term facilities maintenance revenue equals the greater of (1) the sum of (i) $380 times the district's adjusted pupil units times the lesser of one or the ratio of the district's average building age to 35 years, plus (ii) the cost approved by the commissioner for indoor air quality, fire alarm and suppression, and asbestos abatement projects under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.57#stat.123B.57.6">123B.57, subdivision 6</a>, with an estimated cost of $100,000 or more per site, plus (iii) for a school district with an approved voluntary prekindergarten program under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142D.08">142D.08</a>, the cost approved by the commissioner for remodeling existing instructional space to accommodate prekindergarten instruction, or (2) the sum of (i) the amount the district would have qualified for under Minnesota Statutes 2014, section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.57">123B.57</a>, Minnesota Statutes 2014, section 123B.59, and Minnesota Statutes 2014, section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.591">123B.591</a>, and (ii) for a school district with an approved voluntary prekindergarten program under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142D.08">142D.08</a>, the cost approved by the commissioner for remodeling existing instructional space to accommodate prekindergarten instruction.</p>
          <p>(b) Notwithstanding paragraph (a), a school district that qualified for eligibility under Minnesota Statutes 2014, section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.59">123B.59</a>, subdivision 1, paragraph (a), for fiscal year 2010 remains eligible for funding under this section as a district that would have qualified for eligibility under Minnesota Statutes 2014, section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.59">123B.59</a>, subdivision 1, paragraph (a), for fiscal year 2017 and later.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.595.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Long-term facilities maintenance revenue for a charter school.</span></h2>
          <p> Long-term facilities maintenance revenue for a charter school equals $132 times the adjusted pupil units.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.595.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Intermediate districts and other cooperative units.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Upon approval through the adoption of a resolution by each member district school board of an intermediate district or other cooperative unit under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123A.24#stat.123A.24.2">123A.24, subdivision 2</a>, or a joint powers district under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/471.59">471.59</a>, and the approval of the commissioner of education, a school district may include in its authority under this section a proportionate share of the long-term maintenance costs of the intermediate district, cooperative unit, or joint powers district. The cooperative unit or joint powers district may issue bonds to finance the project costs or levy for the costs using long-term maintenance revenue transferred from member districts to make debt service payments or pay project costs or, for leased facilities, pay the portion of lease costs attributable to the amortized cost of long-term facilities maintenance projects completed by the landlord. Authority under this subdivision is in addition to the authority for individual district projects under subdivision 1.</p>
          <p>(b) The resolution adopted under paragraph (a) may specify which member districts will share the project costs under this subdivision, except that debt service payments for bonds issued by a cooperative unit or joint powers district to finance long-term maintenance project costs must be the responsibility of all member districts.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.595.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Facilities plans.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) To qualify for revenue under this section, a school district or intermediate district, not including a charter school, must have a ten-year facility plan adopted by the school board and approved by the commissioner. The plan must include provisions for implementing a health and safety program that complies with health, safety, and environmental regulations and best practices, including indoor air quality management and remediation of lead hazards. For planning purposes, the plan must also address provisions for providing a gender-neutral single-user restroom at each school site.</p>
          <p>(b) The district must annually update the plan, submit the plan to the commissioner for approval by July 31, and indicate whether the district will issue bonds to finance the plan or levy for the costs.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) For school districts issuing bonds to finance the plan, the plan must include a debt service schedule demonstrating that the debt service revenue required to pay the principal and interest on the bonds each year will not exceed the projected long-term facilities revenue for that year.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.595.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Bond authorization.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A school district may issue general obligation bonds under this section to finance facilities plans approved by its board and the commissioner. Chapter 475, except sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/475.58">475.58</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/475.59">475.59</a>, must be complied with. The authority to issue bonds under this section is in addition to any bonding authority authorized by this chapter or other law. The amount of bonding authority authorized under this section must be disregarded in calculating the bonding or net debt limits of this chapter, or any other law other than section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/475.53#stat.475.53.4">475.53, subdivision 4</a>.</p>
          <p>(b) At least 20 days before the earliest of the issuance of bonds or the final certification of levies under subdivision 6, the district must publish notice of the intended projects, the amount of the bond issue, and the total amount of district indebtedness.</p>
          <p>(c) The portion of revenue under this section for bonded debt must be recognized in the debt service fund.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.595.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Levy authorization.</span></h2>
          <p>A district may levy for costs related to an approved plan under subdivision 4 as follows:</p>
          <p>(1) if the district has indicated to the commissioner that bonds will be issued, the district may levy for the principal and interest payments on outstanding bonds issued under subdivision 5 after reduction for any aid receivable under subdivision 9;</p>
          <p>(2) if the district has indicated to the commissioner that the plan will be funded through levy, the district may levy according to the schedule approved in the plan after reduction for any aid receivable under subdivision 9; or</p>
          <p class="in">(3) if the debt service revenue for a district required to pay the principal and interest on bonds issued under subdivision 5 exceeds the district's long-term facilities maintenance revenue for the same fiscal year, the district's general fund levy must be reduced by the amount of the excess.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.595.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Long-term facilities maintenance equalization revenue.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A district's long-term facilities maintenance equalization revenue equals the lesser of (1) $380 times the adjusted pupil units, or (2) the district's revenue under subdivision 1.</p>
          <p>(b) Notwithstanding paragraph (a), a district's long-term facilities maintenance equalization revenue must not be less than the lesser of the district's long-term facilities maintenance revenue or the amount of aid the district received for fiscal year 2015 under Minnesota Statutes 2014, section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.59#stat.123B.59.6">123B.59, subdivision 6</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.595.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Long-term facilities maintenance equalized levy.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A district's long-term facilities maintenance equalized levy equals the district's long-term facilities maintenance equalization revenue minus the greater of:</p>
          <p>(1) the lesser of the district's long-term facilities maintenance equalization revenue or the amount of aid the district received for fiscal year 2015 under Minnesota Statutes 2014, section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.59#stat.123B.59.6">123B.59, subdivision 6</a>; or</p>
          <p>(2) the district's long-term facilities maintenance equalization revenue times the greater of (i) zero, or (ii) one minus the ratio of its adjusted net tax capacity per adjusted pupil unit in the year preceding the year the levy is certified to 123 percent of the state average adjusted net tax capacity per adjusted pupil unit for all school districts in the year preceding the year the levy is certified.</p>
          <p>(b) For purposes of this subdivision, "adjusted net tax capacity" means the value described in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/126C.01#stat.126C.01.2">126C.01, subdivision 2</a>, paragraph (b).</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.595.8a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8a.<span class="headnote">Long-term facilities maintenance unequalized levy.</span></h2>
          <p> A district's long-term facilities maintenance unequalized levy equals the difference between the district's revenue under subdivision 1 and the district's equalization revenue under subdivision 7.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.595.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Long-term facilities maintenance equalized aid.</span></h2>
          <p> A district's long-term facilities maintenance equalized aid equals its long-term facilities maintenance equalization revenue minus its long-term facilities maintenance equalized levy times the ratio of the actual equalized amount levied to the permitted equalized levy.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.595.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">Allowed uses for long-term facilities maintenance revenue.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A district may use revenue under this section for any of the following:</p>
          <p>(1) deferred capital expenditures and maintenance projects necessary to prevent further erosion of facilities;</p>
          <p>(2) increasing accessibility of school facilities;</p>
          <p>(3) health and safety capital projects under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.57">123B.57</a>;</p>
          <p>(4) remodeling or constructing a gender-neutral single-user restroom at each school site; or</p>
          <p>(5) by board resolution, to transfer money from the general fund reserve for long-term facilities maintenance to the debt redemption fund to pay the amounts needed to meet, when due, principal and interest on general obligation bonds issued under subdivision 5.</p>
          <p>(b) A charter school may use revenue under this section for any purpose related to the school.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.595.11">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 11.<span class="headnote">Restrictions on long-term facilities maintenance revenue.</span></h2>
          <p>Notwithstanding subdivision 10, long-term facilities maintenance revenue may not be used:</p>
          <p>(1) for the construction of new facilities, remodeling of existing facilities, or the purchase of portable classrooms, except for the costs associated with constructing or remodeling existing facilities to include at least one gender-neutral single-user restroom authorized under subdivision 10;</p>
          <p>(2) to finance a lease purchase agreement, installment purchase agreement, or other deferred payments agreement;</p>
          <p>(3) for energy-efficiency projects under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.65">123B.65</a>, for a building or property or part of a building or property used for postsecondary instruction or administration, or for a purpose unrelated to elementary and secondary education; or</p>
          <p>(4) for violence prevention and facility security, ergonomics, or emergency communication devices.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.595.12">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 12.<span class="headnote">Reserve account.</span></h2>
          <p>The portion of long-term facilities maintenance revenue not recognized under subdivision 5, paragraph (c), must be maintained in a reserve account within the general fund.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.123B.595.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/1/3/#laws.6.4.0">1Sp2015 c 3 art 6 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/158/#laws.1.42.0">2016 c 158 art 1 s 42</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/189/#laws.30.6.0">2016 c 189 art 30 s 6</a>-13; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/1/5/#laws.5.3.0">1Sp2017 c 5 art 5 s 3</a>,4; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/6/#laws.8.3.0">1Sp2019 c 6 art 8 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/55/#laws.8.1.0">2023 c 55 art 8 s 1</a>-10; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/80/#laws.4.26.0">2024 c 80 art 4 s 26</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/115/#laws.16.42.0">2024 c 115 art 16 s 42</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.123B.60">
        <h1 class="shn">123B.60 BUILDING BONDS FOR CALAMITIES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.60.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Bonds.</span></h2>
          <p>When a building owned by a district is substantially damaged by an act of God or other means beyond the control of the district, the district may issue general obligation bonds without an election to provide money immediately to carry out its adopted long-term facilities maintenance program. Each year the district must pledge an attributable share of its long-term facilities maintenance revenue to the repayment of principal and interest on the bonds. The pledged revenue must be recognized in the debt redemption fund of the district. The district must submit to the department the repayment schedule for any bonds issued under this section. The district must deposit in the debt redemption fund all proceeds received for specific costs for which the bonds were issued, including but not limited to:</p>
          <p>(1) insurance proceeds;</p>
          <p>(2) restitution proceeds; and</p>
          <p>(3) proceeds of litigation or settlement of a lawsuit.</p>
          <p>Before bonds are issued, the district must submit an amended application to the commissioner for long-term facilities maintenance revenue, according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.595">123B.595</a>. The commissioner shall complete all procedures concerning the combined application within 20 days of receiving the application. The publication provisions of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.71#stat.123B.71.12">123B.71, subdivision 12</a>, do not apply to bonds issued under this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.60.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/189/#laws.30.26.0">2016 c 189 art 30 s 26</a>]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.123B.60.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/70/#laws.0.1.0">1989 c 70 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/647/#laws.14.1.0">1994 c 647 art 14 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/3/#laws.16.13.0">1Sp1995 c 3 art 16 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.7.36.0">1998 c 397 art 7 s 36</a>,164; art 11 s 3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/189/#laws.30.14.0">2016 c 189 art 30 s 14</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.123B.61">
        <h1 class="shn">123B.61 PURCHASE OF CERTAIN EQUIPMENT.</h1>
        <p class="in">The board of a district may issue general obligation certificates of indebtedness or capital notes subject to the district debt limits to: (a) purchase vehicles, computers, telephone systems, cable equipment, photocopy and office equipment, technological equipment for instruction, and other capital equipment having an expected useful life at least as long as the terms of the certificates or notes; (b) purchase computer hardware and software, without regard to its expected useful life, whether bundled with machinery or equipment or unbundled, together with application development services and training related to the use of the computer; and (c) prepay special assessments. The certificates or notes must be payable in not more than 20 years and must be issued on the terms and in the manner determined by the board. The certificates or notes may be issued by resolution and without the requirement for an election. The certificates or notes are general obligation bonds for purposes of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/126C.55">126C.55</a>. A tax levy must be made for the payment of the principal and interest on the certificates or notes, in accordance with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/475.61">475.61</a>, as in the case of bonds. The sum of the tax levies under this section and section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.62">123B.62 </a>for each year must not exceed the lesser of the amount of the district's total operating capital revenue or the sum of the district's levy in the general and community service funds excluding the adjustments under this section for the year preceding the year the initial debt service levies are certified. The district's general fund levy for each year must be reduced by the sum of (1) the amount of the tax levies for debt service certified for each year for payment of the principal and interest on the certificates or notes issued under this section as required by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/475.61">475.61</a>, (2) the amount of the tax levies for debt service certified for each year for payment of the principal and interest on bonds issued under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.62">123B.62</a>, and (3) any excess amount in the debt redemption fund used to retire bonds, certificates, or notes issued under this section or section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.62">123B.62</a> after April 1, 1997, other than amounts used to pay capitalized interest. If the district's general fund levy is less than the amount of the reduction, the balance shall be deducted first from the district's community service fund levy, and next from the district's general fund or community service fund levies for the following year. A district using an excess amount in the debt redemption fund to retire the certificates or notes shall report the amount used for this purpose to the commissioner by July 15 of the following fiscal year. A district having an outstanding capital loan under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/126C.69">126C.69</a> must not use an excess amount in the debt redemption fund to retire the certificates or notes.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.123B.61.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/718/#laws.8.6.0">1988 c 718 art 8 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/222/#laws.0.26.0">1989 c 222 s 26</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/3/#laws.5.5.0">1Sp1995 c 3 art 5 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/412/#laws.5.3.0">1996 c 412 art 5 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/1/4/#laws.4.9.0">1Sp1997 c 4 art 4 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.7.37.0">1998 c 397 art 7 s 37</a>,164; art 11 s 3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/241/#laws.4.9.0">1999 c 241 art 4 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/379/#laws.1.43.0">2002 c 379 art 1 s 43</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/154/#laws.10.2.0">2008 c 154 art 10 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2022/0/55/#laws.1.29.0">2022 c 55 art 1 s 29</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/64/#laws.12.2.0">2023 c 64 art 12 s 2</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.123B.62">
        <h1 class="shn">123B.62 BONDS FOR CERTAIN CAPITAL FACILITIES.</h1>
        <p class="in">(a) In addition to other bonding authority, with approval of the commissioner, a district may issue general obligation bonds for certain capital projects under this section. The bonds must be used only to make capital improvements including:</p>
        <p class="in">(1) under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/126C.10#stat.126C.10.14">126C.10, subdivision 14</a>, total operating capital revenue uses specified in clauses (4), (6), (7), (8), (9), and (10);</p>
        <p class="in">(2) the cost of energy modifications;</p>
        <p class="in">(3) improving disability accessibility to school buildings;</p>
        <p class="in">(4) bringing school buildings into compliance with life and safety codes and fire codes; and</p>
        <p class="in">(5) modifying buildings and equipment for security.</p>
        <p class="in">(b) Before a district issues bonds under this subdivision, it must publish notice of the intended projects, the amount of the bond issue, and the total amount of district indebtedness.</p>
        <p class="in">(c) A bond issue tentatively authorized by the board under this subdivision becomes finally authorized unless a petition signed by more than 15 percent of the registered voters of the district is filed with the school board within 30 days of the board's adoption of a resolution stating the board's intention to issue bonds. The percentage is to be determined with reference to the number of registered voters in the district on the last day before the petition is filed with the board. The petition must call for a referendum on the question of whether to issue the bonds for the projects under this section. The approval of 50 percent plus one of those voting on the question is required to pass a referendum authorized by this section.</p>
        <p class="in">(d) The bonds must be paid off within 15 years of issuance. The bonds must be issued in compliance with chapter 475, except as otherwise provided in this section. A tax levy must be made for the payment of principal and interest on the bonds in accordance with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/475.61">475.61</a>. The sum of the tax levies under this section and section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.61">123B.61</a> for each year must not exceed the limit specified in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.61">123B.61</a>. The levy for each year must be reduced as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.61">123B.61</a>. A district using an excess amount in the debt redemption fund to retire the bonds shall report the amount used for this purpose to the commissioner by July 15 of the following fiscal year. A district having an outstanding capital loan under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/126C.69">126C.69</a> must not use an excess amount in the debt redemption fund to retire the bonds.</p>
        <p class="in">(e) Notwithstanding paragraph (d), bonds issued by a district within the first five years following voter approval of a combination according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123A.37#stat.123A.37.2">123A.37, subdivision 2</a>, must be paid off within 20 years of issuance. All the other provisions and limitation of paragraph (d) apply.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.123B.62.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/224/#laws.5.11.0">1993 c 224 art 5 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/3/#laws.5.6.0">1Sp1995 c 3 art 5 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/412/#laws.5.4.0">1996 c 412 art 5 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/1/4/#laws.4.10.0">1Sp1997 c 4 art 4 s 10</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.7.38.0">1998 c 397 art 7 s 38</a>,164; art 11 s 3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/379/#laws.1.44.0">2002 c 379 art 1 s 44</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/56/#laws.0.1.0">2005 c 56 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/363/#laws.2.5.0">2008 c 363 art 2 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2022/0/55/#laws.1.30.0">2022 c 55 art 1 s 30</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.123B.63">
        <h1 class="shn">123B.63 CAPITAL PROJECT REFERENDUM.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.63.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Creation of capital project referendum account.</span></h2>
          <p>A district may create a capital project referendum account as a separate account in its general fund or its building construction fund. All proceeds from the capital project levy must be deposited in the capital project referendum account in its general fund. The portion of the proceeds to be used for building construction must be transferred to the capital project referendum account in its building construction fund. Interest income attributable to the capital project referendum account must be credited to the account.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.63.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Uses of account.</span></h2>
          <p>Money in the capital project referendum account must be used only for the purposes specified in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/126C.10#stat.126C.10.14">126C.10, subdivision 14</a>, for operating capital revenue, including the costs of acquisition and betterment for a project that has been reviewed under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.71">123B.71</a> and has been approved according to subdivision 3.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.63.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Capital project levy referendum.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) A district may levy the local tax rate approved by a majority of the electors voting on the question to provide funds for an approved project. The election must take place no more than five years before the estimated date of commencement of the project. The referendum must be held on a date authorized by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/205A.05#stat.205A.05.1a">205A.05, subdivision 1a</a>. A district must meet the requirements of section 123B.71 for projects funded under this section. If a review and comment is required under section 123B.71, subdivision 8, a referendum for a project not receiving a positive review and comment by the commissioner must be approved by at least 60 percent of the voters at the election.</p>
          <p>(b) The referendum may be called by the school board and may be held:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) separately, before an election for the issuance of obligations for the project under chapter 475; or</p>
          <p class="in">(2) in conjunction with an election for the issuance of obligations for the project under chapter 475; or</p>
          <p class="in">(3) notwithstanding section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/475.59">475.59</a>, as a conjunctive question authorizing both the capital project levy and the issuance of obligations for the project under chapter 475. Any obligations authorized for a project may be issued within five years of the date of the election.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) The ballot must provide a general description of the proposed project, state the estimated total cost of the project, state whether the project has received a positive or negative review and comment from the commissioner, state the maximum amount of the capital project levy as a percentage of net tax capacity, state the amount that will be raised by that local tax rate in the first year it is to be levied, and state the maximum number of years that the levy authorization will apply.</p>
          <p class="in">The ballot must contain a textual portion with the information required in this section and a question stating substantially the following:</p>
          <p class="in">"Shall the capital project levy proposed by the board of .......... School District No. .......... be approved?"</p>
          <p class="in">If approved, the amount provided by the approved local tax rate applied to the net tax capacity for the year preceding the year the levy is certified may be certified for the number of years, not to exceed ten, approved.</p>
          <p>(d) If the district proposes a new capital project to begin at the time the existing capital project expires and at the same maximum tax rate, the general description on the ballot may state that the capital project levy is being renewed and that the tax rate is not being increased from the previous year's rate. An election to renew authority under this paragraph may be called at any time that is otherwise authorized by this subdivision. The ballot notice required under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/275.60">275.60</a> may be modified to read:</p>
          <p>"BY VOTING YES ON THIS BALLOT QUESTION, YOU ARE VOTING TO RENEW AN EXISTING CAPITAL PROJECTS REFERENDUM THAT IS SCHEDULED TO EXPIRE."</p>
          <p class="in">(e) In the event a conjunctive question proposes to authorize both the capital project levy and the issuance of obligations for the project, appropriate language authorizing the issuance of obligations must also be included in the question.</p>
          <p class="in">(f) The district must notify the commissioner of the results of the referendum.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.63.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Excess levy proceeds.</span></h2>
          <p>Any funds remaining in the capital project referendum account that are not applied to the payment of the costs of the approved project before its final completion must be transferred to the district's debt redemption fund.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.123B.63.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/718/#laws.8.18.0">1988 c 718 art 8 s 18</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/719/#laws.5.84.0">1988 c 719 art 5 s 84</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/329/#laws.13.4.0">1989 c 329 art 13 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/1/1/#laws.2.11.0">1Sp1989 c 1 art 2 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/3/#laws.16.13.0">1Sp1995 c 3 art 16 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.7.95.0">1998 c 397 art 7 s 95</a>,96,164; art 11 s 3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/241/#laws.4.29.0">1999 c 241 art 4 s 29</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/464/#laws.3.9.0">2000 c 464 art 3 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/489/#laws.5.19.0">2000 c 489 art 5 s 19</a>,27; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/9/#laws.4.13.0">1Sp2003 c 9 art 4 s 13</a>-16; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/5/#laws.4.8.0">1Sp2005 c 5 art 4 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/146/#laws.4.8.0">2007 c 146 art 4 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/1/11/#laws.1.11.0">1Sp2011 c 11 art 1 s 11</a>; art 4 s 3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/0/92/#laws.2.4.0">2017 c 92 art 2 s 4</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.123B.64"><b>123B.64</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/241/#laws.4.29.0">1999 c 241 art 4 s 29</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.123B.65">
        <h1 class="shn">123B.65 ENERGY-EFFICIENCY PROJECTS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.65.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p>The definitions in this subdivision apply to this section.</p>
          <p>(a) "Energy conservation measure" means a training program or facility alteration designed to reduce energy consumption or operating costs and includes:</p>
          <p>(1) insulation of the building structure and systems within the building;</p>
          <p>(2) storm windows and doors, caulking or weatherstripping, multiglazed windows and doors, heat absorbing or heat reflective glazed and coated window and door systems, additional glazing, reductions in glass area, and other window and door system modifications that reduce energy consumption;</p>
          <p>(3) automatic energy control systems;</p>
          <p>(4) heating, ventilating, or air conditioning system modifications or replacements;</p>
          <p>(5) replacement or modifications of lighting fixtures to increase the energy efficiency of the lighting system without increasing the overall illumination of a facility, unless such increase in illumination is necessary to conform to the applicable state or local building code for the lighting system after the proposed modifications are made;</p>
          <p>(6) energy recovery systems;</p>
          <p>(7) cogeneration systems that produce steam or forms of energy such as heat, as well as electricity, for use primarily within a building or complex of buildings;</p>
          <p>(8) energy conservation measures that provide long-term operating cost reductions.</p>
          <p>(b) "Guaranteed energy-savings contract" means a contract for the evaluation and recommendations of energy conservation measures, and for one or more energy conservation measures. The contract must provide that all payments, except obligations on termination of the contract before its expiration, are to be made over time, but not to exceed 15 years from the date of final installation, and the savings are guaranteed to the extent necessary to make payments for the systems.</p>
          <p>(c) "Qualified provider" means a person or business experienced in the design, implementation, and installation of energy conservation measures. A qualified provider to whom the contract is awarded shall give a sufficient bond to the school district for its faithful performance.</p>
          <p>(d) "Commissioner" means the commissioner of commerce through the state energy office.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.65.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Energy-efficiency contract.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) Notwithstanding any law to the contrary, a school district may enter into a guaranteed energy-savings contract with a qualified provider to significantly reduce energy or operating costs.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) Before entering into a contract under this subdivision, the board shall comply with clauses (1) to (5).</p>
          <p class="in">(1) The board must seek proposals from multiple qualified providers by publishing notice of the proposed guaranteed energy-savings contract in the board's official newspaper and in other publications if the board determines that additional publication is necessary to notify multiple qualified providers.</p>
          <p class="in">(2) The school board must select the qualified provider that best meets the needs of the board. The board must provide public notice of the meeting at which it will select the qualified provider.</p>
          <p class="in">(3) The contract between the board and the qualified provider must describe the methods that will be used to calculate the costs of the contract and the operational and energy savings attributable to the contract.</p>
          <p class="in">(4) The qualified provider shall issue a report to the board giving a description of all costs of installations, modifications, or remodeling, including costs of design, engineering, installation, maintenance, repairs, or debt service, and giving detailed calculations of the amounts by which energy or operating costs will be reduced and the projected payback schedule in years.</p>
          <p class="in">(5) The board must provide published notice of the meeting in which it proposes to award the contract, the names of the parties to the proposed contract, and the contract's purpose.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) The board must provide a copy of any contract entered into under paragraph (a) and the report provided under paragraph (b), clause (4), to the commissioner of commerce within 30 days of the effective date of the contract.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.65.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Evaluation by commissioner.</span></h2>
          <p>Upon request of the board, the commissioner shall review the report required in subdivision 2 and provide an evaluation to the board on the proposed contract within 15 working days of receiving the report. In evaluating the proposed contract, the commissioner shall determine whether the detailed calculations of the costs and of the energy and operating savings are accurate and reasonable. The commissioner may request additional information about a proposed contract as the commissioner deems necessary. If the commissioner requests additional information, the commissioner shall not be required to submit an evaluation to the board within fewer than ten working days of receiving the requested information.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.65.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Review of savings under contract.</span></h2>
          <p>Upon request of the board, the commissioner shall conduct a review of the energy and operating cost savings realized under a guaranteed energy-savings contract every three years during the period a contract is in effect. The commissioner shall compare the savings realized under the contract during the period under review with the calculations of savings included in the report required under subdivision 2 and provide an evaluation to the board concerning the performance of the system and the accuracy and reasonableness of the claimed energy and operating cost savings.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.65.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Payment of review expenses.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may charge a district requesting services under subdivisions 3 and 4 actual costs incurred by the Department of Commerce while conducting the review, or one-half percent of the total identified project cost, whichever is less. Before conducting the review, the commissioner shall notify a district requesting review services that expenses will be charged to the district. The commissioner shall bill the district upon completion of the contract review. Money collected by the commissioner under this subdivision must be deposited in the general fund. A district may include the cost of a review by the commissioner under subdivision 3 in a contract made pursuant to this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.65.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Contract provisions.</span></h2>
          <p>Guaranteed energy-savings contracts that include a written guarantee that savings will meet or exceed the cost of energy conservation measures is not subject to competitive bidding requirements. The contract is not subject to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.52">123B.52</a> or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/471.345">471.345</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.65.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">District action.</span></h2>
          <p>A district may enter into a guaranteed energy-savings contract with a qualified provider if, after review of the report and the commissioner's evaluation if requested, the board finds that the amount it would spend on the energy conservation measures recommended in the report is not likely to exceed the amount to be saved in energy and operation costs over 15 years from the date of installation if the recommendations in the report were followed, and the qualified provider provides a written guarantee that the energy or operating cost savings will meet or exceed the costs of the system. The guaranteed energy-savings contract may provide for payments over a period of time, not to exceed 15 years. Notwithstanding section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.79">123B.79</a>, a district annually may transfer from the general fund to the reserve for operating capital account an amount up to the amount saved in energy and operation costs as a result of guaranteed energy-savings contracts.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.65.8">
