      <div class="section" id="stat.123B.885">
        <h1 class="shn">123B.885 DIESEL SCHOOL BUSES; OPERATION OF ENGINE; PARKING.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.885.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Operation of engine.</span></h2>
          <p>All operators of diesel school buses must minimize, to the extent practical, the idling of school bus engines and exposure of children to diesel exhaust fumes.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.885.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Parking.</span></h2>
          <p>On and after July 1, 2003, diesel school buses must be parked and loaded at sufficient distance from school air-intake systems to avoid diesel fumes from being drawn into the systems, unless, in the judgment of the school board, alternative locations block traffic, impair student safety, or are not cost effective.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.123B.885.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/364/#laws.0.2.0">2002 c 364 s 2</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.123B.89"><b>123B.89</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/241/#laws.1.69.0">1999 c 241 art 1 s 69</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.123B.90">
        <h1 class="shn">123B.90 SCHOOL BUS SAFETY TRAINING.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.90.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/9/#laws.10.14.0">1Sp2003 c 9 art 10 s 14</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.90.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Student training.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) Each district must provide public school pupils enrolled in kindergarten through grade 10 with age-appropriate school bus safety training, as described in this section, of the following concepts:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) transportation by school bus is a privilege and not a right;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) district policies for student conduct and school bus safety;</p>
          <p class="in">(3) appropriate conduct while on the school bus;</p>
          <p class="in">(4) the danger zones surrounding a school bus;</p>
          <p class="in">(5) procedures for safely boarding and leaving a school bus;</p>
          <p class="in">(6) procedures for safe street or road crossing; and</p>
          <p class="in">(7) school bus evacuation.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) Each nonpublic school located within the district must provide all nonpublic school pupils enrolled in kindergarten through grade 10 who are transported by school bus at public expense and attend school within the district's boundaries with training as required in paragraph (a).</p>
          <p class="in">(c) Students enrolled in kindergarten through grade 6 who are transported by school bus and are enrolled during the first or second week of school must receive the school bus safety training competencies by the end of the third week of school. Students enrolled in grades 7 through 10 who are transported by school bus and are enrolled during the first or second week of school and have not previously received school bus safety training must receive the training or receive bus safety instructional materials by the end of the sixth week of school. Students taking driver's training instructional classes must receive training in the laws and proper procedures when operating a motor vehicle in the vicinity of a school bus as required by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/169.446">169.446, subdivisions 2</a> and 3. Students enrolled in kindergarten through grade 10 who enroll in a school after the second week of school and are transported by school bus and have not received training in their previous school district shall undergo school bus safety training or receive bus safety instructional materials within four weeks of the first day of attendance. Upon request of the superintendent of schools, the school transportation safety director in each district must certify to the superintendent that all students transported by school bus within the district have received the school bus safety training according to this section. Upon request of the superintendent of the school district where the nonpublic school is located, the principal or other chief administrator of each nonpublic school must certify to the school transportation safety director of the district in which the school is located that the school's students transported by school bus at public expense have received training according to this section.</p>
          <p class="in">(d) A district and a nonpublic school with students transported by school bus at public expense may provide kindergarten pupils with bus safety training before the first day of school.</p>
          <p class="in">(e) A district and a nonpublic school with students transported by school bus at public expense must make reasonable accommodations for the school bus safety training of pupils known to speak English as a second language and pupils with disabilities.</p>
          <p class="in">(f) The district and a nonpublic school with students transported by school bus at public expense must provide students enrolled in kindergarten through grade 3 school bus safety training twice during the school year.</p>
          <p class="in">(g) A district and a nonpublic school with students transported by school bus at public expense must conduct a school bus evacuation drill at least once during the school year.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.90.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Model training program.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall develop and maintain a comprehensive list of school bus safety training instructional materials for pupils who ride the bus that includes bus safety curriculum for both classroom and practical instruction.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.123B.90.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/647/#laws.12.4.0">1994 c 647 art 12 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/3/#laws.2.6.0">1Sp1995 c 3 art 2 s 6</a>,7; art 16 s 13; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/412/#laws.2.7.0">1996 c 412 art 2 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/1/4/#laws.12.3.0">1Sp1997 c 4 art 12 s 3</a>,4; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.7.6.0">1998 c 397 art 7 s 6</a>,7,164; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/241/#laws.9.22.0">1999 c 241 art 9 s 22</a>,23; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/489/#laws.6.13.0">2000 c 489 art 6 s 13</a>,14; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/9/#laws.1.6.0">1Sp2003 c 9 art 1 s 6</a>,7; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/294/#laws.2.13.0">2004 c 294 art 2 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/146/#laws.8.2.0">2007 c 146 art 8 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/0/103/#laws.0.2.0">2011 c 103 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/68/#laws.4.11.0">2023 c 68 art 4 s 11</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.123B.91">
        <h1 class="shn">123B.91 SCHOOL DISTRICT BUS SAFETY RESPONSIBILITIES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.91.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Comprehensive policy.</span></h2>
          <p>Each district shall develop and implement a comprehensive, written policy governing pupil transportation safety, including transportation of nonpublic school students, when applicable. The policy, at minimum, must contain:</p>
          <p>(1) provisions for appropriate student bus safety training under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.90">123B.90</a>;</p>
          <p>(2) rules governing student conduct on school buses and in school bus loading and unloading areas;</p>
          <p>(3) a statement of parent or guardian responsibilities relating to school bus safety;</p>
          <p>(4) an intradistrict system for reporting school bus accidents or misconduct and a system for dealing with local law enforcement officials in cases of criminal conduct on a school bus;</p>
          <p>(5) a discipline policy to address violations of school bus safety rules, including procedures for revoking a student's bus riding privileges in cases of serious or repeated misconduct;</p>
          <p>(6) a system for integrating school bus misconduct records with other discipline records;</p>
          <p>(7) where applicable, provisions governing bus monitor qualifications, training, and duties;</p>
          <p>(8) rules governing the use and maintenance of type III vehicles, drivers of type III vehicles, qualifications to drive a type III vehicle, qualifications for a type III vehicle, and the circumstances under which a student may be transported in a type III vehicle;</p>
          <p>(9) operating rules and procedures;</p>
          <p>(10) emergency procedures;</p>
          <p>(11) a system for maintaining and inspecting equipment; and</p>
          <p>(12) any other requirements of the school district.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.91.1a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 1a.<span class="headnote">Compliance by nonpublic and charter school students.</span></h2>
          <p>A nonpublic or charter school student transported by a public school district shall comply with student bus conduct and student bus discipline policies of the transporting public school district.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.91.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">School transportation safety director.</span></h2>
          <p>Each board shall designate a school transportation safety director to oversee and implement pupil transportation safety policies. The director shall have day-to-day responsibility for pupil transportation safety within the district, including transportation of nonpublic school children when provided by the district.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.123B.91.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/647/#laws.12.14.0">1994 c 647 art 12 s 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/3/#laws.2.8.0">1Sp1995 c 3 art 2 s 8</a>,9; art 16 s 13; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.7.9.0">1998 c 397 art 7 s 9</a>,164; art 11 s 3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/398/#laws.6.21.0">1998 c 398 art 6 s 21</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/241/#laws.9.24.0">1999 c 241 art 9 s 24</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/489/#laws.6.15.0">2000 c 489 art 6 s 15</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/9/#laws.1.8.0">1Sp2003 c 9 art 1 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2006/0/263/#laws.2.10.0">2006 c 263 art 2 s 10</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.123B.92">
        <h1 class="shn">123B.92 TRANSPORTATION AID ENTITLEMENT.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.92.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">For purposes of this section and section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/125A.76">125A.76</a>, the terms defined in this subdivision have the meanings given to them.</p>
          <p class="in">(a) "Actual expenditure per pupil transported in the regular and excess transportation categories" means the quotient obtained by dividing:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) the sum of:</p>
          <p class="in">(i) all expenditures for transportation in the regular category, as defined in paragraph (b), clause (1), and the excess category, as defined in paragraph (b), clause (2), plus</p>
          <p class="in">(ii) an amount equal to one year's depreciation on the district's school bus fleet and mobile units computed on a straight line basis at the rate of 15 percent per year for districts operating a program under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.128">124D.128</a> for grades 1 to 12 for all students in the district and 12-1/2 percent per year for other districts of the cost of the fleet, plus</p>
          <p class="in">(iii) an amount equal to one year's depreciation on the district's type III vehicles, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/169.011#stat.169.011.71">169.011, subdivision 71</a>, which must be used a majority of the time for pupil transportation purposes, computed on a straight line basis at the rate of 20 percent per year of the cost of the type three school buses by:</p>
          <p class="in">(2) the number of pupils eligible for transportation in the regular category, as defined in paragraph (b), clause (1), and the excess category, as defined in paragraph (b), clause (2).</p>
          <p class="in">(b) "Transportation category" means a category of transportation service provided to pupils as follows:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) "Regular transportation" is:</p>
          <p class="in">(i) transportation to and from school during the regular school year for resident elementary pupils residing one mile or more from the public or nonpublic school they attend, and resident secondary pupils residing two miles or more from the public or nonpublic school they attend, excluding desegregation transportation and noon kindergarten transportation; but with respect to transportation of pupils to and from nonpublic schools, only to the extent permitted by sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.84">123B.84</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.87">123B.87</a>;</p>
          <p class="in">(ii) transportation of resident pupils to and from language immersion programs;</p>
          <p class="in">(iii) transportation of a pupil who is a custodial parent and that pupil's child between the pupil's home and the child care provider and between the provider and the school, if the home and provider are within the attendance area of the school;</p>
          <p class="in">(iv) transportation to and from or board and lodging in another district, of resident pupils of a district without a secondary school;</p>
          <p class="in">(v) transportation to and from school during the regular school year required under subdivision 3 for nonresident elementary pupils when the distance from the attendance area border to the public school is one mile or more, and for nonresident secondary pupils when the distance from the attendance area border to the public school is two miles or more, excluding desegregation transportation and noon kindergarten transportation; and</p>
          <p>(vi) transportation of pregnant or parenting pupils to and from a program that was established on or before January 1, 2018, or that is in operation on or after July 1, 2021, that provides:</p>
          <p>(A) academic instruction;</p>
          <p>(B) at least four hours per week of parenting instruction; and</p>
          <p class="in">(C) high-quality child care on site during the education day with the capacity to serve all children of enrolled pupils.</p>
          <p class="in">For the purposes of this paragraph, a district may designate a licensed day care facility, school day care facility, respite care facility, the residence of a relative, or the residence of a person or other location chosen by the pupil's parent or guardian, or an after-school program for children operated by a political subdivision of the state, as the home of a pupil for part or all of the day, if requested by the pupil's parent or guardian, and if that facility, residence, or program is within the attendance area of the school the pupil attends.</p>
          <p class="in">(2) "Excess transportation" is:</p>
          <p class="in">(i) transportation to and from school during the regular school year for resident secondary pupils residing at least one mile but less than two miles from the public or nonpublic school they attend, and transportation to and from school for resident pupils residing less than one mile from school who are transported because of full-service school zones, extraordinary traffic, drug, or crime hazards; and</p>
          <p class="in">(ii) transportation to and from school during the regular school year required under subdivision 3 for nonresident secondary pupils when the distance from the attendance area border to the school is at least one mile but less than two miles from the public school they attend, and for nonresident pupils when the distance from the attendance area border to the school is less than one mile from the school and who are transported because of full-service school zones, extraordinary traffic, drug, or crime hazards.</p>
          <p class="in">(3) "Desegregation transportation" is transportation within and outside of the district during the regular school year of pupils to and from schools located outside their normal attendance areas under a plan for desegregation mandated by the commissioner or under court order.</p>
          <p class="in">(4) "Transportation services for pupils with disabilities" is:</p>
          <p class="in">(i) transportation of pupils with disabilities who cannot be transported on a regular school bus between home or a respite care facility and school;</p>
          <p class="in">(ii) necessary transportation of pupils with disabilities from home or from school to other buildings, including centers such as developmental achievement centers, hospitals, and treatment centers where special instruction or services required by sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/125A.03">125A.03 </a>to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/125A.24">125A.24</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/125A.26">125A.26</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/125A.48">125A.48</a>, and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/125A.65">125A.65</a> are provided, within or outside the district where services are provided;</p>
          <p class="in">(iii) necessary transportation for resident pupils with disabilities required by sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/125A.12">125A.12</a>, and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/125A.26">125A.26</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/125A.48">125A.48</a>;</p>
          <p class="in">(iv) board and lodging for pupils with disabilities in a district maintaining special classes;</p>
          <p class="in">(v) transportation from one educational facility to another within the district for resident pupils enrolled on a shared-time basis in educational programs, and necessary transportation required by sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/125A.18">125A.18</a>, and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/125A.26">125A.26</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/125A.48">125A.48</a>, for resident pupils with disabilities who are provided special instruction and services on a shared-time basis or if resident pupils are not transported, the costs of necessary travel between public and private schools or neutral instructional sites by essential personnel employed by the district's program for children with a disability;</p>
          <p class="in">(vi) transportation for resident pupils with disabilities to and from board and lodging facilities when the pupil is boarded and lodged for educational purposes;</p>
          <p>(vii) transportation of pupils for a curricular field trip activity on a school bus equipped with a power lift when the power lift is required by a student's disability or section 504 plan; and</p>
          <p>(viii) services described in items (i) to (vii), when provided for pupils with disabilities in conjunction with a summer instructional program that relates to the pupil's individualized education program or in conjunction with a learning year program established under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.128">124D.128</a>.</p>
          <p class="in">For purposes of computing special education initial aid under section 125A.76, the cost of providing transportation for children with disabilities includes (A) the additional cost of transporting a student in a shelter care facility as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.007">260C.007, subdivision </a>30, a student placed in a family foster home as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.007">260C.007, subdivision </a>16b, a homeless student in another district to the school of origin, or a formerly homeless student from a permanent home in another district to the school of origin but only through the end of the academic year; and (B) depreciation on district-owned school buses purchased after July 1, 2005, and used primarily for transportation of pupils with disabilities, calculated according to paragraph (a), items (ii) and (iii). Depreciation costs included in the disabled transportation category must be excluded in calculating the actual expenditure per pupil transported in the regular and excess transportation categories according to paragraph (a). For purposes of subitem (A), a school district may transport a child who does not have a school of origin to the same school attended by that child's sibling, if the siblings are homeless or in a shelter care facility.</p>
          <p class="in">(5) "Nonpublic nonregular transportation" is:</p>
          <p class="in">(i) transportation from one educational facility to another within the district for resident pupils enrolled on a shared-time basis in educational programs, excluding transportation for nonpublic pupils with disabilities under clause (4);</p>
          <p class="in">(ii) transportation within district boundaries between a nonpublic school and a public school or a neutral site for nonpublic school pupils who are provided pupil support services pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.44">123B.44</a>; and</p>
          <p class="in">(iii) late transportation home from school or between schools within a district for nonpublic school pupils involved in after-school activities.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) "Mobile unit" means a vehicle or trailer designed to provide facilities for educational programs and services, including diagnostic testing, guidance and counseling services, and health services. A mobile unit located off nonpublic school premises is a neutral site as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.41#stat.123B.41.13">123B.41, subdivision 13</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.92.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/241/#laws.1.69.0">1999 c 241 art 1 s 69</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.92.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Alternative attendance programs.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) A district that enrolls nonresident pupils in programs under sections<a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123A.05"> 123A.05</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123A.08">123A.08</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.03">124D.03</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.08">124D.08</a>, and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.68">124D.68</a>, must provide authorized transportation to the pupil within the attendance area for the school that the pupil attends at the same level of service that is provided to resident pupils within the attendance area. The resident district need not provide or pay for transportation between the pupil's residence and the district's border.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) A district may provide transportation to allow a student who attends a high-need English learner program and who resides within the transportation attendance area of the program to continue in the program until the student completes the highest grade level offered by the program.</p>
          <p>(c) A homeless nonresident pupil enrolled under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.08#stat.124D.08.2a">124D.08, subdivision 2a</a>, must be provided transportation from the pupil's district of residence to and from the school of enrollment.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.92.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/241/#laws.1.69.0">1999 c 241 art 1 s 69</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.92.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">District reports.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Each district must report data to the department as required by the department to account for transportation expenditures.</p>
          <p>(b) Salaries and fringe benefits of district employees whose primary duties are other than transportation, including central office administrators and staff, building administrators and staff, teachers, social workers, school nurses, and instructional aides, must not be included in a district's transportation expenditures, except that a district may include salaries and benefits according to paragraph (c) for (1) an employee designated as the district transportation director, (2) an employee providing direct support to the transportation director, or (3) an employee providing direct transportation services such as a bus driver or bus aide.</p>
          <p>(c) Salaries and fringe benefits of the district employees listed in paragraph (b), clauses (1), (2), and (3), who work part time in transportation and part time in other areas must not be included in a district's transportation expenditures unless the district maintains documentation of the employee's time spent on pupil transportation matters in the form and manner prescribed by the department.</p>
          <p>(d) A school district that contracts for transportation service may allocate transportation expense to transportation categories based upon contract rates. Districts may only allocate transportation expense to transportation categories based upon contract rates if contract rates are reasonably consistent on a cost-per-hour, cost-per-mile, cost-per-route, or cost-per-student basis. In order to allocate transportation expense based upon contract rates, a school district, if audited, must be able to demonstrate to the auditor that variances in the application of transportation cost basis rates are appropriate.</p>
          <p>(e) Pupil transportation expenditures, excluding expenditures for capital outlay, leased buses, student board and lodging, crossing guards, and aides on buses, may be allocated among transportation categories based on cost-per-mile or cost-per-student regardless of whether the transportation services are provided on district-owned or contractor-owned school buses. Expenditures for school bus driver salaries and fringe benefits may either be directly charged to the appropriate transportation category or may be allocated among transportation categories based on cost-per-mile or cost-per-student. Expenditures by private contractors or individuals who provide transportation exclusively in one transportation category must be charged directly to the appropriate transportation category. Transportation services provided by contractor-owned school bus companies incorporated under different names but owned by the same individual or group of individuals must be treated as the same company for cost allocation purposes.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.92.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/241/#laws.1.69.0">1999 c 241 art 1 s 69</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.92.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/241/#laws.1.69.0">1999 c 241 art 1 s 69</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.92.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/241/#laws.1.69.0">1999 c 241 art 1 s 69</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.92.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Nonpublic pupil transportation aid.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A district's nonpublic pupil transportation aid for the 1996-1997 and later school years for transportation services for nonpublic school pupils according to sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.88">123B.88</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.84">123B.84</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.86">123B.86</a>, and this section, equals the sum of the amounts computed in paragraphs (b) and (c). This aid does not limit the obligation to transport pupils under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.84">123B.84</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.87">123B.87</a>.</p>
          <p>(b) For regular and excess transportation according to subdivision 1, paragraph (b), clauses (1) and (2), an amount equal to the product of:</p>
          <p>(1) the district's actual expenditure per pupil transported in the regular and excess transportation categories during the second preceding school year; times</p>
          <p>(2) the number of nonpublic school pupils residing in the district who receive regular or excess transportation service or reimbursement for the current school year; times</p>
          <p>(3) the ratio of the formula allowance pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/126C.10#stat.126C.10.2">126C.10, subdivision 2</a>, for the current school year to the formula allowance pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/126C.10#stat.126C.10.2">126C.10, subdivision 2</a>, for the second preceding school year.</p>
          <p>(c) For nonpublic nonregular transportation according to subdivision 1, paragraph (b), clause (5), an amount equal to the product of:</p>
          <p>(1) the district's actual expenditure for nonpublic nonregular transportation during the second preceding school year; times</p>
          <p>(2) the ratio of the formula allowance pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/126C.10#stat.126C.10.2">126C.10, subdivision 2</a>, for the current school year to the formula allowance pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/126C.10#stat.126C.10.2">126C.10, subdivision 2</a>, for the second preceding school year.</p>
          <p>(d) Notwithstanding the amount of the formula allowance for fiscal years 2015 and 2016 in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/126C.10#stat.126C.10.2">126C.10, subdivision 2</a>, the commissioner shall use the amount of the formula allowance for the current year minus $414 in determining the nonpublic pupil transportation revenue in paragraphs (b) and (c) for fiscal years 2015 and 2016.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.92.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/241/#laws.1.69.0">1999 c 241 art 1 s 69</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.92.11">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 11.<span class="headnote">Area learning center transportation aid.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A district or cooperative unit under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123A.24#stat.123A.24.2">123A.24, subdivision 2</a>, that provides transportation of pupils to and from an area learning center program established under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123A.05">123A.05</a> is eligible for state aid to reimburse the additional costs of transportation during the preceding fiscal year.</p>
          <p>(b) A district or cooperative unit under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123A.24#stat.123A.24.2">123A.24, subdivision 2</a>, may apply to the commissioner of education for state aid to reimburse the costs of transporting pupils who are enrolled in an area learning center program established under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123A.05">123A.05 </a>during the preceding fiscal year. The commissioner shall develop the form and manner of applications for state aid, the criteria to determine when transportation is necessary, and the accounting procedure to determine excess costs. In determining aid amounts, the commissioner shall consider other revenue received by the district or cooperative unit under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123A.24#stat.123A.24.2">123A.24, subdivision 2</a>, for transportation for area learning center purposes.</p>
          <p>(c) The total aid entitlement for this section is $1,000,000 each year. The commissioner must prorate aid if this amount is insufficient to reimburse costs for a district or cooperative unit under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123A.24#stat.123A.24.2">123A.24, subdivision 2</a>.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.123B.92.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1979/0/334/#laws.2.8.0">1979 c 334 art 2 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1980/0/609/#laws.2.2.0">1980 c 609 art 2 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/356/#laws.0.167.0">1981 c 356 s 167</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/358/#laws.2.3.0">1981 c 358 art 2 s 3</a>-13; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/1/2/#laws.0.8.0">1Sp1981 c 2 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/3/2/#laws.2.9.0">3Sp1981 c 2 art 2 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1982/0/548/#laws.2.3.0">1982 c 548 art 2 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/314/#laws.2.2.0">1983 c 314 art 2 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/1/12/#laws.2.3.0">1Sp1985 c 12 art 2 s 3</a>-10; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/1/1/#laws.9.7.0">1Sp1986 c 1 art 9 s 7</a>,8; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/398/#laws.2.2.0">1987 c 398 art 2 s 2</a>-7; art 7 s 42; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/486/#laws.0.35.0">1988 c 486 s 35</a>,36; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/718/#laws.2.5.0">1988 c 718 art 2 s 5</a>; art 7 s 29; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/222/#laws.0.14.0">1989 c 222 s 14</a>-25; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/329/#laws.2.3.0">1989 c 329 art 2 s 3</a>,9; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/562/#laws.2.5.0">1990 c 562 art 2 s 5</a>-7; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/130/#laws.0.37.0">1991 c 130 s 37</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/199/#laws.2.10.0">1991 c 199 art 2 s 10</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/265/#laws.2.6.0">1991 c 265 art 2 s 6</a>-14; art 3 s 38; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/277/#laws.0.17.0">1991 c 277 s 17</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/499/#laws.12.29.0">1992 c 499 art 12 s 29</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/224/#laws.2.5.0">1993 c 224 art 2 s 5</a>-9; art 7 s 10,11; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/647/#laws.2.2.0">1994 c 647 art 2 s 2</a>; art 3 s 24; art 12 s 8,9; art 13 s 7; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/3/#laws.2.11.0">1Sp1995 c 3 art 2 s 11</a>-24; art 4 s 30; art 16 s 13; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/412/#laws.2.9.0">1996 c 412 art 2 s 9</a>-12; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/1/4/#laws.1.19.0">1Sp1997 c 4 art 1 s 19</a>-24; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/1/5/#laws.1.7.0">1Sp2017 c 5 art 1 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/11/#laws.1.6.0">1Sp2019 c 11 art 1 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/55/#laws.1.5.0">2023 c 55 art 1 s 5</a>;       <div class="section" id="stat.123B.93">
        <h1 class="shn">123B.93 ADVERTISING ON SCHOOL BUSES.</h1>
        <p>(a) The commissioner, through a competitive process, may contract with advertisers regarding advertising on school buses. At a minimum, the contract must prohibit advertising and advertising images that:</p>
        <p>(1) solicit the sale of, or promote the use of, alcoholic beverages and tobacco products;</p>
        <p>(2) are discriminatory in nature or content;</p>
        <p>(3) imply or declare an endorsement of the product or service by the school district;</p>
        <p>(4) contain obscene material;</p>
        <p>(5) are false, misleading, or deceptive; or</p>
        <p>(6) relate to an illegal activity or antisocial behavior.</p>
        <p>(b) Advertisement must meet the following conditions:</p>
        <p>(1) the advertising attached to the school bus does not interfere with bus identification under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/169.441">169.441</a>; and</p>
        <p>(2) the bus with attached advertising meets the school bus equipment standards under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/169.4501">169.4501</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/169.4504">169.4504</a>.</p>
        <p>(c) All buses operated by school districts may be attached with advertisements under the state contract. All school district contracts shall include a provision for advertisement. Each school district shall be reimbursed by the advertiser for all costs incurred by the district and its contractors for supporting the advertising program, including, but not limited to, retrofitting buses, storing advertising, attaching advertising to the bus, and related maintenance.</p>
        <p>(d) The commissioner shall hold harmless and indemnify each district for all liabilities arising from the advertising program. Each district must tender defense of all such claims to the commissioner within five days of receipt.</p>
        <p>(e) All revenue from the contract shall be deposited in the general fund.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.123B.93.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/1/4/#laws.12.1.0">1Sp1997 c 4 art 12 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.4.51.0">1998 c 397 art 4 s 51</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/9/#laws.12.7.0">1Sp2003 c 9 art 12 s 7</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.123B.935">
        <h1 class="shn">123B.935 ACTIVE TRANSPORTATION SAFETY TRAINING.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.935.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Training required.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Each district must provide public school pupils enrolled in kindergarten through grade 3 with age-appropriate active transportation safety training. At a minimum, the training must include pedestrian safety, including crossing roads.</p>
          <p>(b) Each district must provide public school pupils enrolled in grades 4 through 8 with age-appropriate active transportation safety training. At a minimum, the training must include:</p>
          <p>(1) pedestrian safety, including crossing roads safely using the searching left, right, left for vehicles in traffic technique;</p>
          <p>(2) bicycle safety, including relevant traffic laws, use and proper fit of protective headgear, bicycle parts and safety features, and safe biking techniques; and</p>
          <p>(3) electric-assisted bicycle safety, including that a person under the age of 15 is not allowed to operate an electric-assisted bicycle.</p>
          <p>(c) A nonpublic school may provide nonpublic school pupils enrolled in kindergarten through grade 8 with training as specified in paragraphs (a) and (b).</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.935.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Deadlines.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) Students under subdivision 1, paragraph (a), who are enrolled during the first or second week of school and have not previously received active transportation safety training specified in that paragraph must receive the safety training by the end of the third week of school.</p>
          <p>(b) Students under subdivision 1, paragraph (b), who are enrolled during the first or second week of school and have not previously received active transportation safety training specified in that paragraph must receive the safety training by the end of the sixth week of school.</p>
          <p>(c) Students under subdivision 1, paragraph (a) or (b), who enroll in a school after the second week of school and have not received the appropriate active transportation safety training in their previous school district must undergo the training or receive active transportation safety instructional materials within four weeks of the first day of attendance.</p>
          <p>(d) A district and a nonpublic school may provide kindergarten pupils with active transportation safety training before the first day of school.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.935.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Instruction.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A district may provide active transportation safety training through distance learning.</p>
          <p>(b) A district and a nonpublic school must make reasonable accommodations for the active transportation safety training of pupils known to speak English as a second language and pupils with disabilities.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.935.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Model program.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of transportation must maintain a comprehensive collection of active transportation safety training materials that meets the requirements under this section.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.123B.935.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/68/#laws.4.12.0">2023 c 68 art 4 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/127/#laws.3.8.0">2024 c 127 art 3 s 8</a></p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">COMMON SCHOOL DISTRICTS;<br>PRINSBURG AND FRANCONIA</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.123B.94">
        <h1 class="shn">123B.94 COMMON SCHOOL DISTRICTS; MEETINGS, ELECTIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.94.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Annual meeting.</span></h2>
          <p>The annual meeting of all common districts must be held on the last Tuesday in June, at 8:00 p.m., unless a different hour has been fixed at the preceding annual meeting. The clerk shall give notice of the meeting, specifying the matters to come before such meeting. Failure of the clerk to give such notice, or to specify the business to be transacted, shall not affect the validity of any business, except the raising of money to build or purchase a school house, the authorizing of an issue of bonds, the fixing of a school house site, or the organization as an independent district. At the annual meeting in a common district five legal voters shall constitute a quorum. The chair and clerk of the board shall officiate in their respective capacities at all meetings of the electors of the district. In the event of the absence of the chair or clerk, the voters shall elect a chair or clerk pro tem. The voters shall have the power in an annual meeting to repeal and modify their proceedings. The polls at all meetings shall be open at least one hour.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.94.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Elections.</span></h2>
          <p>The annual meeting must have power to elect, by ballot, officers of the district. In all elections or vote by ballot, the clerk shall record the names of all voters participating therein and the chair shall appoint two electors. The electors, with the assistance of the clerk, shall supervise the balloting and canvass the votes. If any candidates receive an equal number of votes for an office, the board shall resolve the tie by lot.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.94.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Candidates for office.</span></h2>
          <p>Any person desiring to be a candidate for a district office at the annual meeting of the district shall file with the clerk of the district an application to be placed on the ballot for such office. Any five voters of the district may file an application for or on behalf of any qualified voter in the district that they desire to be a candidate. Applications shall be filed not less than 12 days before the annual district meeting. The clerk of the district, in the notice of annual meeting, shall state the names of the candidates for whom applications have been filed, failure to do so shall not affect the validity of the election thereafter held. At the annual meeting of common districts, nomination of candidates for offices may be made from the floor by any qualified voter.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.94.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Ballots.</span></h2>
          <p>The clerk shall prepare, at the expense of the district, necessary ballots for the election of officers placing thereon the names of the proposed candidates for such office with a blank space after such names. The ballots shall be marked as official ballots, and shall be used to the exclusion of all other ballots at such annual meeting or election in the election of officers of the district.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.94.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Election of officers.</span></h2>
          <p>At the first meeting of each district, the chair shall be elected to hold office until July 1 following the next annual meeting. The treasurer, until one year from such date, and the clerk until two years from such date.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.94.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Board member acceptance of office.</span></h2>
          <p>A board member elected at an annual meeting upon notice from the clerk, shall, on or before the first Saturday in July, file with the clerk an acceptance of the office and an official oath. Any person appointed by the board or elected at a special meeting to fill a vacancy shall file in writing an acceptance of the office and an official oath within ten days after the notice of such appointment or election by the clerk. A person who fails to qualify prior to the time specified shall be deemed to have refused to serve, but such filing, if made at any time before action to fill the vacancy has been taken, shall be sufficient.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.94.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Special meeting.</span></h2>
          <p>Upon the filing of a petition therefor, executed by five eligible voters, as defined in Minnesota Election Law, of the common district, specifying the business to be acted upon, or upon the adoption of a proper resolution so specifying, signed by a majority of the members of the board, the clerk shall call a special meeting of the district. The clerk shall give ten days' posted notice and one week's published notice if there be a newspaper printed in the district and specify in the notice the business named in the request or resolution and the time and place of the meeting. If there is no clerk in the district or if the clerk fails for three days after receiving a request or resolution to give notice of a meeting, it may be called by like notice by five eligible voters, as defined in Minnesota Election Law, of the district. No business except that named in the notice shall be transacted at the meeting. If there are not five eligible voters, as defined in Minnesota Election Law, or if there is not a board therein, the county auditor may call a special meeting by giving notice thereof as provided in this section. The voters at a special meeting have power to repeal or modify their proceedings.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.123B.94.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1959/1/71/#laws.4.2.0">Ex1959 c 71 art 4 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1965/0/33/#laws.0.1.0">1965 c 33 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1979/0/29/#laws.0.1.0">1979 c 29 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1980/0/609/#laws.6.15.0">1980 c 609 art 6 s 15</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/266/#laws.2.8.0">1987 c 266 art 2 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.6.37.0">1998 c 397 art 6 s 37</a>-41,124</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.123B.95">
        <h1 class="shn">123B.95 BOARDS OF COMMON SCHOOL DISTRICTS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.95.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">School board.</span></h2>
          <p>The care, management and control of a common district is vested in a board of three members to be known as the school board. The term of office of a member shall be three years, and until a successor qualifies. The board of each common district must consist of a chair, a treasurer, and a clerk. The board may by resolution establish a time and place for regular meeting and no notice of such meeting need be sent to any members of the board.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.95.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Finances.</span></h2>
          <p>The board must submit to the annual meeting an estimate of the expenses of the district for the coming year for a school term as determined by the board and for such other specified purposes as the board may deem proper. If the annual meeting fails to vote a sufficient tax to maintain the district for such time, the board must levy such tax pursuant to and within the limitations of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.22">124D.22</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/126C.40">126C.40</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/126C.45">126C.45</a>, and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/126C.48">126C.48</a>; but no board shall expend any money or incur any liability for any purpose beyond the sum appropriated by vote of the district for such purpose, or levied by the board pursuant to this subdivision, or on hand and applicable thereto.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.95.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">School visits.</span></h2>
          <p>The board must visit each school at least once every three months.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.95.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Official newspaper.</span></h2>
          <p>At its first meeting following July 1 each year, the board must designate, by resolution, as the official newspaper of the district, some legal newspaper of general circulation within the district, and contract with such newspaper for its publications. If there is more than one such newspaper, the board must enter a contract with the lowest responsible bidder at the earliest practicable date. All notices and proceedings required by law to be published by the board must be published in the official newspaper so designated. The fees for such publication must not exceed the fees for publication of legal notices as prescribed by Minnesota Statutes.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.123B.95.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1959/1/71/#laws.4.3.0">Ex1959 c 71 art 4 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1967/0/176/#laws.0.1.0">1967 c 176 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1978/0/706/#laws.0.11.0">1978 c 706 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/130/#laws.0.37.0">1991 c 130 s 37</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/499/#laws.12.29.0">1992 c 499 art 12 s 29</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/212/#laws.4.64.0">1995 c 212 art 4 s 64</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.6.42.0">1998 c 397 art 6 s 42</a>,124; art 11 s 3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/254/#laws.0.16.0">2000 c 254 s 16</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.123B.96">
        <h1 class="shn">123B.96 TREASURER.</h1>
        <p>The treasurer shall receive and be responsible for all money in the district and disburse the same on orders signed by the clerk and countersigned by the chair or other vouchers authorized by law. In the event that the chair has been continuously absent from the district for a period of 30 days or more, the treasurer may pay orders without the signature of the chair. Each order must state the fund on which it is drawn, the name of the payee, and the nature of the claim for which such order is issued and must be so drawn that when signed by the treasurer in an appropriate place, it becomes a check on the district depository. The treasurer shall keep an account of each fund, and of all receipts and disbursements showing the sources of all receipts and the nature and purpose of disbursements. The treasurer shall deposit the funds of the district in the official depository in accordance with the provisions of law.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.123B.96.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1959/1/71/#laws.4.4.0">Ex1959 c 71 art 4 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1965/0/69/#laws.0.1.0">1965 c 69 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/492/#laws.0.7.0">1973 c 492 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.6.43.0">1998 c 397 art 6 s 43</a>,124</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.123B.97">
        <h1 class="shn">123B.97 SCHOOLHOUSES AND SITES; COMMON SCHOOL DISTRICTS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.97.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Acquisition of sites.</span></h2>
          <p>When authorized by the voters at a regular meeting or at a special meeting called for that purpose, the board may acquire necessary sites for school houses, or enlargements or additions to existing school house sites, by lease, purchase, or condemnation under the power of eminent domain; lease, erect or purchase garages for district-owned school buses; and sell or exchange school houses or sites and execute deeds of conveyances thereof. It may acquire by lease, purchase, or condemnation under eminent domain suitable tracts of land either in or outside of the district for the purpose of instruction, experimentation, and demonstration in agriculture. In any city, a school site, when practicable, must contain at least one block. Outside of any city, a school site must contain at least two acres. If any school house site contains less than the required amount the board may, without a vote of the electors, acquire other land adjacent to or near such site to make, with such site, all or part of the required amount. If property is taken by eminent domain by authority of this subdivision, when needed by the district for such purpose, the fact that the property was acquired by the owner under the power of eminent domain, or is already devoted to public use, shall not prevent its acquisition by the district.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.97.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Site designation.</span></h2>
          <p>The annual meeting or election shall have power to designate a site for a school house and provide for building or otherwise placing a school house thereon, when proper notice has been given. A site with an existing school house or where a school house is being built shall not be changed except by vote of three-fifths of the voters of the district voting on the question.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123B.97.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Teacher dwelling.</span></h2>
          <p>When authorized by a two-thirds majority of all the electors voting at an annual or special meeting, the board may erect, purchase, or acquire a dwelling house for the use of its teachers. The proposition shall be submitted only at a meeting or election. The notice of the meeting shall state that the proposition shall be considered or submitted.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.123B.97.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1959/1/71/#laws.4.6.0">Ex1959 c 71 art 4 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/123/#laws.5.7.0">1973 c 123 art 5 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.6.44.0">1998 c 397 art 6 s 44</a>,124; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2006/0/214/#laws.0.20.0">2006 c 214 s 20</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.123B.98">
        <h1 class="shn">123B.98 LIMITATION OF SECTIONS.</h1>
        <p>Material contained in sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.94">123B.94</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.97">123B.97</a> relates only to Common School Districts Numbers 323 and 815. The provisions of law relating to independent school districts shall apply to and govern these common school districts unless a particular provision of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.94">123B.94</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.97">123B.97</a> provides for the matter, in which case that provision shall apply and control.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.123B.98.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1959/1/71/#laws.4.12.0">Ex1959 c 71 art 4 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/162/#laws.0.22.0">1975 c 162 s 22</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1978/0/706/#laws.0.12.0">1978 c 706 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1982/0/424/#laws.0.30.0">1982 c 424 s 30</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.6.124.0">1998 c 397 art 6 s 124</a>; art 11 s 3</p>
      </div>
    </div>
